                                Software version updated.

  1 About This Manual                                                                     1.1 Purpose




                                                 1        About This Manual

1.1 Purpose
                         This manual describes Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC software
                    programming modes, memory mapping, interrupt handling, system boot
                    mode, Cache, also describes the features, block diagrams, functional
                    descriptions, registers, and driver functions of external devices (UART,
                    GPIO, I2C, SPI, RTC, PIT, WDT, DMA, PLMT, SMU, Flash, Ethernet, CAN,
                    SD), also describes DSP software library, NN software library, embedded
                    real-time operating systems (FreeRTOS, uC/OS-III, RT-Thread Nano
                    version, RT-Thread standard version, and Zephyr), TinyML, real-time
                    processing, compiler built-in functions, benchmarking programs, and
                    applications, etc.

                       Table 1-1 shows the abbreviations and terminology used in this
                    manual.

2.2.2 Embedded RTOS Software Programming
                      The Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports the following embedded
                   RTOS software programming method:
                      FreeRTOS
                      uC/OS-III
                      RT-Thread Nano version
                      RT-Thread standard version
                      Zephyr

2.2.3 DSP Software Programming
                       Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports DSP software programming,
                   providing a complete set of DSP software programming API functions with
                   concise code, enabling users to develop DSP software system quickly.
                         DSP software programming API functions include basic operations
                   such as vector, matrix, and complex vector, as well as complex operations
                   like filtering and transformation functions.

2.2.4 NN Software Programming
                       Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports NN software programming,
                   providing API functions implemented in pure C language or optimized for
                   DSP ISA extensions. These functions encapsulate the computational
                   complexity involved in neural network calculations, ensuring concise code
                   and facilitating rapid development of NN software systems.
                       The NN software programming API functions cover activation
                   functions, basic mathematical functions, connection functions, convolution
                   functions, fully connected functions, pooling functions, normalization
                   exponential functions, and other auxiliary utility functions.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                8(346)
  2 Software Programming Mode                                        2.2 Software Programming Mode




2.2.5 Protocol Stack Software Programming
                        Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports Ethernet TCP/IP protocol stack
                   software programming, providing the open-source LwIP protocol stack.
                       The LwIP protocol stack is a lightweight open-source TCP/IP protocol
                   stack capable of running with or without an operating system. It can
                   operate efficiently while minimizing memory usage, maintaining the
                   essential features of the TCP protocol.

2.2.6 TinyML Software Programming
                       Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports TFLite Micro software
                   programming.
                        TFLite Micro (TensorFlow Lite for Microcontrollers) is a lightweight
                   version of TensorFlow Lite, designed specifically for running machine
                   learning models on microcontrollers or other memory-constrained
                   embedded devices.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                9(346)

  4 Interrupt Handling                                                              4.1 Interrupt Handling




                                                      4        Interrupt Handling

4.1 Interrupt Handling
                              Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC interrupt handling includes two modes:
                         local interrupt handling and global interrupt handling. Local interrupt
                         handling refers to interrupts that are directly processed by the AE350
                         RISC-V processor. Global interrupt handling, on the other hand, involves
                         external interrupts that require arbitration through PLIC before entering the
                         AE350 RISC-V processor. The interrupt handling for Gowin
                         RiscV_AE350_SOC is as shown in Table 4-1.
                              AE350 RISC-V processor supports local interrupt handling, which
                         includes non-maskable interrupt (nmi), Machine Timer Interrupt Pending
                         (mtip), and Machine Mode Software Interrupt Pending (msip).
                              AE350 RISC-V processor also supports global interrupt handling,
                         including all external interrupts arbitrated by PLIC (meip and seip).
                         Table 4-1 Interrupt Handling Method
                          Interrupt Handling Method   Description
                          nmi                         WDT interrupt
                          mtip                        Machine timer interrupt
                          msip                        Machine software interrupt
                          meip                        Machine PLIC interrupt
                          seip                        Supervisor PLIC interrupt


4.2 Global Interrupt Handling
                              When Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC instantiates the PLIC module for
                         secondary use, all interrupt sources bound to position 0 are designated as
                         the software interrupt controller (PLIC_SW). PLIC generates interrupt


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                      12(346)
  4 Interrupt Handling                                                           4.2 Global Interrupt Handling



                         signals via programming registers for software interrupt generation.
                              Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC's global interrupt handling includes
                         vectored interrupt and non-vectored interrupt processing. For example, the
                         software programming application ae350_demo uses non-vectored
                         interrupt processing, while ae350_vectored uses vectored interrupt
                         processing. The differences between non-vectored and vectored interrupt
                         processing are located in bsp\ae350\start.S, trap.c, interrupt.h and
                         interrupt.c.
                              The interrupt sources and their corresponding interrupt signals and
                         interrupt handling functions for Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC's global
                         interrupt are as shown in Table 4-2.
                              The interrupt source and interrupt signal definition are located in
                         bsp\ae350\ae350.h. The definition of interrupt handling function is located
                         in bsp\ae350\interrupt.c.
                         Table 4-2 PLIC Global Interrupt Handling
  Interrupt Source         Interrupt signal             Interrupt Handling Function   Description
  1                        IRQ_RTCPERIOD_SOURCE         rtc_period_irq_handler        RTC period
                                                                                      interrupt
  2                        IRQ_RTCALARM_SOURCE          rtc_alarm_irq_handler         RTC alarm interrupt
  3                        IRQ_PIT_SOURCE               pit_irq_handler               PIT interrupt
  4                        IRQ_GP0_SOURCE               gp0_irq_handler               User defined
                                                                                      interrupt
  5                        IRQ_SPI2_SOURCE              spi_irq_handler               SPI2 interrupt
  6                        IRQ_I2C_SOURCE               i2c_irq_handler               I2C interrupt
  7                        IRQ_GPIO_SOURCE              gpio_irq_handler              GPIO interrupt
  8                        IRQ_UART1_SOURCE             uart1_irq_handler             UART1 interrupt
  9                        IRQ_UART2_SOURCE             uart2_irq_handler             UART2 interrupt
  10                       IRQ_DMA_SOURCE               dma_irq_handler               DMAC interrupt
  11                       IRQ_GP1_SOURCE               gp1_irq_handler               User defined
                                                                                      interrupt
  12                       IRQ_GP2_SOURCE               gp2_irq_handler               User defined
                                                                                      interrupt
  13                       IRQ_GP3_SOURCE               gp3_irq_handler               User defined

                                                5         System Boot Mode

                        BUILD_LOAD

                   bsp\config\config.h
                   Table 5-1 System Boot Mode
                       Boot Mode     SaG File                Description
                       BUILD_LOAD    ae350-ilm.sag           The program is loaded in ILM by GDB
                                                             or eBIOS.
                                     ae350-ddr.sag           The program is loaded in DDR/SRAM
                                                             memory by GDB or eBIOS.
                       BUILD_BURN    ae350-ddr.sag           The Program is burned to the
                                                             Flash/ROM memory, but run in
                                                             DDR/SRAM memory.
                                     ae350_rpu_dlm.sag       The program is burned to the
                                                             Flash/ROM memory, but run in DLM.
                                     ae350_rpu_func.sag      The program is burned to the
                                                             Flash/ROM memory, but some key
                                                             codes run in ILM or DLM, others run in
                                                             DDR/SRAM memory.
                                     ae350_rpu_ilm.sag       The program is burned to the
                                                             Flash/ROM memory, but run in ILM.
                                     ae350_rpu_ilm_dlm.sag   The program is burned to the


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                15(346)
  5 System Boot Mode



                       Boot Mode   SaG File            Description
                                                       Flash/ROM memory, but codes run in
                                                       ILM and data in DLM.
                       BUILD_XIP   ae350-xip.sag       The program is burned to the
                                                       Flash/ROM memory, and run in
                                                       Flash/ROM memory.
                                   ae350_rpu_xip.sag   The program is burned to the
                                                       Flash/ROM memory, and codes run in
                                                       Flash/ROM memory and data in DLM.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                          16(346)
  6 Cache                                                                         6.1 Introduction




                                                                        6          Cache

6.1 Introduction
                    Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports L1 Cache, including instruction
               cache (I-Cache) and data cache (D-Cache). I-Cache definition is as shown
               in Table 6-1, and D-Cache definition is as shown in Table 6-2.
                    Cache size = Cache lines per way X Ways X Line size
               Table 6-1 I-Cache Definition
                Properties                    Configuration
                Size                          32 KB
                Cache lines per way           256
                Ways                          Direct-mapped, 4-way
                Lines size (bytes)            32
                Soft Error Protection         ECC, single-error and double error correction
                Replacement Policy            LRU



               Table 6-2 D-Cache Definition
                Properties                    Configuration
                Size                          32 KB
                Cache lines per way           256
                Ways                          Direct-mapped, 4-way
                Lines size (bytes)            32
                Soft Error Protection         ECC, single-error and double error detection
                Replacement Policy            LRU



MUG1029-1.3E                                                                              17(346)
  6 Cache                                                                         6.2 Control Register




6.2 Control Register
                   The definition of Cache control register is as shown in Table 6-3.
               Table 6-3 Cache Control Register
                Name           Location    Description                     Type      Initial Value
                IC_EN          [0]         Controls if the instruction     RW        0
                                           cache is enabled or not.
                                           0: I-Cache is disabled
                                           1: I-Cache is enabled

6.4 Configuration
                   If Cache is enabled, it can accelerate system boot and program
               running.
                     Cache uses the LRU algorithm for instruction prefetch and

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                        20(346)
  6 Cache                                                                   6.4 Configuration



               replacement from main memory. If AE350 RISC-V processor consistently
               hits the prefetch instructions in the Cache, it can improve the speed and
               efficiency of system boot and program running. If AE350 RISC-V
               processor misses prefetch instructions in the Cache, the Cache uses the
               LRU algorithm to replace instructions from main memory.
                   Cache configuration definition is located in bsp\config\config.h and
               bsp\ae350\ae350.c.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                         21(346)
  7 UART                                                                      7.1 Introduction




                                                                      7       UART

7.1 Introduction
                   Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC includes two UART controllers.

7.1.1 Features

                  Accesses registers through the AMBA 2.0 bus interface
                  Supports hardware TX and RX FIFO with 64-byte
                  Supports programmable oversampling rate (8x and 16x)
                  Programmable timing compatible with 16C550A UART
                   -   Supports data widths of 5 to 8 bits per byte
                   -   Supports 1-bit, 1.5-bit, or 2-bit stop bit
                   -   Supports odd, even parity, and fixed parity bit
                   -   Supports hardware flow control (CTS/RTS)
                   -   Supports DMA transfer
                   -   Supports programmable transfer rate
                   -   Supports modem control interface
                   -   Supports comprehensive status reporting
                   -   Supports line break, parity error, frame error, and data overrun
                       detection




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                          22(346)
  7 UART                                                                                 7.2 Register Definition




7.1.2 Block Diagram

                   Figure 7-1 shows the block diagram of UART.
               Figure 7-1 UART Block Diagram


                                                                                 Baud Rate
                 APB Bus               Register File

                                            TX FIFO                                TX Controller

                                            RX FIFO                                RX Controller
                                                                Register


                                                                                                   Modem Interface
                                          Modem Controller

                                     PCLK                                             UCLK
                                    Domain                                           Domain



7.1.3 Functional Description

                    UART is a serial communication controller that provides an
               asynchronous serial interface for external devices or modems. UART
               includes TX module, RX module, baud rate generation module, modem
               control module, as well as register file group and APB bus interface.

7.2 Register Definition

7.2.1 Register Definition

                    The definitions of UART register are as shown in Table 7-1. UART
               register definition is located in bsp\ae350\ae350.h.
               Table 7-1 Register Definition
                Address Offset     Name           Description
                0x00               IDREV          ID and Revision Register
                0x04~0x0C          -              Reserved
                0x10               CFG            Hardware Configuration Register
                0x14               OSCR           Over Sample Control Register
                0x18~0x1C          -              Reserved
                0x20               DLAB = 0

7.2.2 Register Description

                   The following sections describe the UART register definitions in
               details.
                     The register type abbreviations are summarized as follows:
                    RO: Read-only
                    WO: Write-only
                    R/W: Readable and writable
                    W1C: Write 1 to clear
                    RC: Read clear
               ID and Revision Registers (0x00)
                    The ID and Revision registers are used to store ID and Revision
               numbers, and the initial values are dependent on the version used. The
               definitions of ID and Revision registers are shown in Table 7-2.
               Table 7-2 ID and Revision Register

                ID        31:16    RO        ID number for UART            0x0201

                Minor      3:0              RO          Minor revision number      Revision dependent

               Over Sample Control Register (0x14)

               7-4.

                Name     Bit      Type        Description                                              Reset

                                              The value must be an even number; any odd value
                                              writes to this field will be converted to an even
                                              value.
                                              OSC = 0: The over-sample ratio is 32

               Receiver Buffer Register (DLAB = 0) (0x20)

               details. When FIFOE is 1 (FIFO mode), the Receiver Buffer register is

               details. When FIFOE is 1 (FIFO mode), the Transmitter Holding register is

               Divisor Latch LSB (DLAB = 1) (0x20)
                    The Divisor Latch LSB stores the divisor for generating the sample
               clock from the UART clock source (uclk). The size of the Divisor Latch LSB
               is 16 bits (2 bytes) and the register stores the least significant byte of the
               Divisor Latch. The valid value of the Divisor Latch LSB is between 1 and
               65535 (216-1); 1 and 65535 are inclusive.
                    The Divisor Latch LSB is defined as shown in Table 7-8.
               Table 7-8 Divisor Latch LSB (when DLAB = 1)
                Name    Bit     Type     Description                                          Reset
                -       31:8    -        Reserved                                             0x0
                DLL     7:0     R/W      Least significant byte of the Divisor Latch          0x1



               Divisor Latch MSB (DLAB = 1) (0x24)
                   The Divisor Latch MSB stores the divisor for generating the sample
               clock from the UART clock source (uclk). The size of the Divisor Latch
               MSB is 16 bits (2 bytes) and the register stores the most significant byte of
               the Devisor Latch MSB. The valid value of the Divisor Latch MSB is
               between 1 and 65535 (216-1); 1 and 65535 are inclusive.
                    The Divisor Latch MSB is defined as shown in Table 7-9.
               Table 7-9 Divisor Latch MSB (when DLAB = 1)
                Name    Bit     Type      Description                                         Reset
                -       31:8    -         Reserved                                            0x0
                DLM     7:0     R/W       Most significant byte of the Divisor Latch          0x0

               Line Status Register (0x34)
                   Line Status register, which reports the status of the transmitter and
               receiver. The definition of Line Status register is shown in Table 7-18.
               Table 7-18 Line Status Register
                Name     Bit    Type   Description                                               Reset
                -        31:8   -      Reserved                                                  0x0
                ERRF     7      RO     Error in RXFIFO                                           0x0
                                       In the FIFO mode, this bit is set when there is at
                                       least one parity error, framing error, or line break
                                       associated with data in the RXFIFO. It is cleared
                                       when this register is read and there is no more
                                       error for the rest of data in the RXFIFO
                TEMT     6      RO     Transmitter empty                                         0x1
                                       This bit is 1 when the THR (TXFIFO in the FIFO
                                       mode) and the Transmitter Shift Register (TSR)
                                       are both empty. Otherwise, it is zero.
                THRE     5      RO     Transmitter Holding Register empty                        0x1
                                       This bit is 1 when the THR (TXFIFO in the FIFO
                                       mode) is empty. Otherwise, it is zero.
                                       If the THRE interrupt is enabled, an interrupt is
                                       triggered when THRE becomes 1.
                LBreak   4      RO     Line break                                                0x0
                                       This bit is set when the uart_sin input signal was
                                       held LOW for longer than the time for a full-word
                                       transmission. A full-word transmission is the
                                       transmission of the START, data, parity, and STOP
                                       bits. It is cleared when this register is read.
                                       In the FIFO mode, this bit indicates the line break
                                       for the received data at the top of the RXFIFO.
                FE       3      RO     Framing error                                             0x0
                                       This bit is set when the received STOP bit is not

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                    33(346)
  7 UART                                                                          7.2 Register Definition



                Name     Bit    Type   Description                                               Reset
                                       HIGH. It is cleared when this register is read.
                                       In the FIFO mode, this bit indicates the framing
                                       error for the received data at the top of the
                                       RXFIFO.
                PE       2      RO     Parity error                                              0x0
                                       This bit is set when the received parity does not
                                       match with the parity selected in the LCR [5:4]. It is
                                       cleared when this register is read.
                                       In the FIFO mode, this bit indicates the parity error
                                       for the received data at the top of the RXFIFO.
                OE       1      RO     Overrun error                                             0x0
                                       This bit indicates that data in the Receiver Buffer
                                       Register (RBR) is overrun.
                DR       0      RO     Data ready                                                0x0
                                       This bit is set when there are incoming received
                                       data in the Receiver Buffer Register (RBR). It is
                                       cleared when all of the received data are read.



               Modem Status Register (0x38)
                     The definitions of Modem Status register are shown in Table 7-19.
               Table 7-19 Modem Status Register
                Name     Bit    Type   Description                                               Reset
                -        31:8   -      Reserved                                                  0x0
                DCD      7      RO     Data carrier detect                                       0x0
                                       0: The modem_dcdn input signal is HIGH.
                                       1: The modem_dcdn input signal is LOW.
                RI       6      RO     Ring indicator                                            0x0
                                       0: The modem_rin input signal is HIGH.
                                       1: The modem_rin input signal is LOW.
                DSR      5      RO     Data set ready                                            0x0
                                       0: The modem_dsrn input signal is HIGH.
                                       1: The modem_dsrn input signal is LOW.
                CTS      4      RO     Clear to send                                             0x0
                                       0: The modem_ctsn signal is HIGH.
                                       1: The modem_ctsn signal is LOW.


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                    34(346)
  7 UART                                                                7.3 Driver Function Definition



                Name    Bit    Type   Description                                             Reset
                DDCD    3      RC     Delta data carrier detect                               0x0
                                      This bit is set when the state of the modem_dcdn
                                      input signal has been changed since the last time
                                      this register is read. Otherwise, it is zero.
                TERI    2      RC     Trailing edge ring indicator                            0x0
                                      This bit is set when the state of the modem_rin
                                      input signal has been changed from LOW to HIGH
                                      since the last time this register is read.
                DDSR    1      RC     Delta data set ready                                    0x0
                                      This bit is set when the state of the modem_dsrn
                                      input signal has been changed since the last time
                                      this register is read.
                DCTS    0      RC     Delta clear to send                                     0x0
                                      This bit is set when the state of the modem_ctsn
                                      input signal has been changed since the last time
                                      this register is read.

  8 GPIO                                                                     8.1 Introduction




                                                                     8        GPIO

8.1 Introduction
                    Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC includes a GPIO controller that supports
               up to 32 independently programmable input and output channels.

8.1.1 Features

                  Supports AMBA 2.0 APB bus protocol specification
                  Up to 32 independent I/O channels
                  Programmable pull-up and pull-down per channel
                  Programmable debounce per input channel
                  Each input channel can be configured as an interrupt input source
                   -   Interrupt source can be configured as level-triggered or edge-
                       triggered

8.1.2 Block Diagram

                   Figure 8-1 shows the block diagram of GPIO.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                         44(346)
  8 GPIO                                                                           8.2 Register Definition



               Figure 8-1 GPIO Block Diagram




                                                                       gpio_pulldown
                                                     Channel       gpio_pullup
                                                                        gpio_oe
                                                     Control
                                                      Logic           gpio_out


                         APB           Control         De-
                                                                      gpio_in

                       Interface       Register      bounce                      P AD

                                                      Logic                  ex tc lk


                                                    Interrupt
                                                    Detection                gpio_int r

                                                      Logic




8.1.3 Functional Description

                    The GPIO controller provides up to 32 independent I/O channels,
               each of which can be independently configured as an input or output port.
               If used as an input port, the controller can sample the input signal and
               generate an interrupt. If the controller is used as an output port, the output
               signal can be driven by a pull-up or pull-down.
                    The GPIO controller also supports debounce function that filters out
               glitches from the input channels, which can be configured to be enabled
               individually for each channel, and the debounce duration is also
               configurable.

                    The definitions of GPIO register are as shown in Table 8-1. GPIO
               register definition is located in bsp\ae350\ae350.h.

                  RO: Read-only

                  W1C: Write 1 to clear

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                     58(346)
  9 I2C                                                                    9.1 Introduction




                                                                       9          I2C

9.1 Introduction
                   Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC contains an I2C controller that supports
               both master and slave modes.

9.1.1 Features

                  Supports AMBA 2.0 APB bus protocol specification
                  Supports standard mode (100 Kb/s), fast mode (400 Kb/s) and ultra-
                   fast mode (1 Mb/s)
                  Programming master and slave modes
                  Supports 7-bit and 10-bit addressing mode
                  Supports General Broadcast Address
                  Automatic clock stretching
                  Programmable clock and data timing
                  Supports DMA transfer

9.1.2 Block Diagram

                   The I2C block diagram is as shown in Figure 9-1.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                       59(346)
  9 I2C                                                                                 9.2 Register Definition



               Figure 9-1 I2C Structure Block Diagram


                                                                                 Glitch           SDA input
                    APB                                                       Suppression
                                                    Control logics/State         Filter           SCL input
                                 Register File
                                                        machines
                                                                                                  SDA output
               Interrupt
                                                                                                  SCL output



                                           DMA acknowledge      DMA request


9.1.3 Functional Description

                    The I2C controller can be configured as either I2C master or slave
               mode by setting the control registers. If used as I2C master mode, the
               controller provides an efficient way to initiate I2C transactions; if used as
               I2C slave mode, the controller is addressed when the address byte of an
               I2C transaction matches an address register.
                    I2C provides a Timing Parameter Multiplier Register (TPM) to enable
               the I2C to meet the timing requirements of the I2C bus when the I2C is
               running at a relatively fast APB clock frequency. The actual values of all
               timing parameters in the Setup register are multiplied by (TPM+1).

9.2 Register Definition

9.2.1 Register Definition

                    The I2C register description is as shown in Table 9-1. The I2C register
               definition is located in bsp\ae350\ae350.h.

                0x14                   INTEN         Interrupt Enable Register
                0x18                   STATUS        Status Register
                0x1C                   ADDR          Address Register
                0x20                   DATA          Data Register
                0x24                   CTRL          Control Register

9.2.2 Register Description

                   The following sections describe the I2C register definitions in details.
               The register definitions differ when the I2C controller is used as a master
               mode or a slave mode and are described separately below.
                     The register type abbreviations are summarized as follows:
                    RO: Read-only
                    WO: Write-only (read as zero)
                    R/W: Readable and writable
                    R/W1C: Readable and Write 1 to clear
               ID and Revision Register (0x00)
                    The ID and Revision registers are used to store ID and Revision
               numbers, and the initial values are dependent on the version used. The
               definition of ID and Revision registers are shown in Table 9-2.
               Table 9-2 ID and Revision Register
                Name         Bit        Type     Description                 Reset
                ID           31:8       RO       ID number for I2C           0x020210
                RevMajor     7:4        RO       Major revision number       Revision Dependent
                RevMinor     3:0        RO       Minor revision number       Revision Dependent



               Configuration Register (0x10)
                   The Configuration register is used to store the size of the data FIFO.
               The definition of Configuration register is shown in Table 9-3.
               Table 9-3 Configuration Register
                Name             Bit      Type     Description       Reset
                –                31:2     –        Reserved          –
                FIFOSize         1:0      RO       FIFO size:        Configuration Dependent
                                                   0: 2 bytes
                                                   1: 4 bytes


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                   61(346)
  9 I2C                                                                              9.2 Register Definition



                Name          Bit        Type      Description        Reset
                                                   2: 8 bytes
                                                   3: 16 bytes



               Interrupt Enable Register (0x14)

               The Interrupt Enable register definition is shown in Table 9-4.
               Table 9-4 Interrupt Enable Register
                Name         Bit     Type       Description                                         Reset
                –            31:10   –          Reserved                                            –
                Cmpl         9       R/W        Set to enable the Completion Interrupt.             0x0

                ByteTrans    7       R/W        Set to enable the Byte Transmit Interrupt.          0x0

                ArbLose      4       R/W        Set to enable the Arbitration Lose Interrupt.       0x0
                                                Master: interrupts when the controller loses
                                                the bus arbitration.

                                                returned an ACK.
                                                Slave: interrupts when the controller is

                                            Transmitter: Interrupts when the FIFO is half-

               Address Register (0x1C)
                    The Address register is used to hold the slave address. When I2C is
               configured as master mode, this is the target slave address for the next
               transaction; when I2C is configured as slave mode, this is the address of
               the controller on the bus.
                    The definition of Address register is shown in Table 9-6.
               Table 9-6 Address Register
                Name    Bit         Type    Description                                Reset
                –       31:10       –       Reserved                                   –
                Addr    9:0         R/W     The slave address.                         0x0
                                            For 7-bit addressing mode, the most
                                            significant 3 bits are ignored and
                                            only the least-significant 7 bits of
                                            Addr are valid.



               Data Register (0x20)

               Table 9-7 Data Register

                Data    7:0     R/W        Write this register to put one byte of data to the             0x0

                                           Read this register to get one byte of data from the
                                           FIFO.

               Control Register (0x24)

               phase and record the process of data phase. The definition of Control
               register is shown in Table 9-8.
               Table 9-8 Control Register
                Name          Bit     Type   Description                                       Reset
                –             31:13   –      Reserved                                          -
                Phase_start   12      R/W    Enable this bit to send a START condition at      0x1
                                             the beginning of transaction.
                                             Master mode only.
                Phase_addr    11      R/W    Enable this bit to send the address after         0x1
                                             START condition.

                                             Address phase.
                                             Master mode only.

                                             Master: Set this bit to determine the
                                             direction for the next transaction.
                                             0: Transmitter
                                             1: Receiver
                                             Slave: The direction of the last received
                                             transaction.
                                             0: Receiver
                                             1: Transmitter
                DataCnt       7:0     R/W    Data counts in bytes.                             0x0
                                             Master: The number of bytes to
                                             transmit/receive. 0 means 256 bytes.
                                             DataCnt will be decreased by one for each
                                             byte transmitted/received.
                                             Slave: the meaning of DataCnt depends on
                                             the DMA mode:
                                             If DMA is not enabled, DataCnt is the
                                             number of bytes transmitted/received from
                                             the bus master. It is reset to 0 when the

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                  66(346)
  9 I2C                                                                          9.2 Register Definition



                Name          Bit    Type   Description                                         Reset
                                            controller is addressed and then increased
                                            by one for each byte of data
                                            transmitted/received.
                                            If DMA is enabled, DataCnt is the number of
                                            bytes to transmit/receive. It will not be reset
                                            to 0 when the slave is addressed and it will
                                            be decreased by one for each byte of data
                                            transmitted/received.



               Command Register (0x28)
                    The definition of Command register is shown in Table 9-9.
               Table 9-9 Command Register
                Name   Bit    Type   Description                                                Reset
                –      31:3   –      Reserved                                                   –
                CMD    2:0    R/W    Write this register with the following values to           0x0
                                     perform the corresponding actions:
                                     0: no action
                                     1: issue a data transaction (Master only)
                                     2: respond with an ACK to the received byte
                                     3: respond with a NACK to the received byte
                                     4: clear the FIFO
                                     5: reset the I2C controller (abort current transaction,
                                     set the SDA and SCL line to the open-drain mode,
                                     reset the Status Register and the Interrupt Enable
                                     Register, and empty the FIFO)
                                     When issuing a data transaction by writing 0x1 to
                                     this register, the CMD field stays at 0x1 for the
                                     duration of the entire transaction, and it is only
                                     cleared to 0x0 after when the transaction has
                                     completed or when the controller loses the
                                     arbitration.
                                     Note: No transaction will be issued by the controller
                                     when all phases (Start, Address, Data and Stop)
                                     are disabled.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                   67(346)
  9 I2C                                                                           9.2 Register Definition



               Setup Register (0x2C)
                    The Setup register is used to store the programmable configuration
               and timing parameters for the I2C bus. For the timing details, you can see
               9.3 Timing Setting.
                    The definition of Setup register is shown in Table 9-10.
               Table 9-10 Setup Register
                Name          Bit     Type   Description                                         Reset
                –             31:29   –      Reserved                                            –
                T_SUDAT       28:24   R/W    T_SUDAT defines the data setup time                 0x5
                                             before releasing the SCL.
                                             Setup time = (2 * tpclk) + (2 + T_SP +
                                             T_SUDAT) * tpclk * (TPM + 1)
                                             tpclk = PCLK period
                                             TPM = The multiplier value in Timing
                                             Parameter Multiplier Register
                T_SP          23:21   R/W    T_SP defines the pulse width of spikes that         0x1
                                             must be suppressed by the input filter.
                                             Pulse width = T_SP * tpclk * (TPM + 1)
                T_HDDAT       20:16   R/W    T_HDDAT defines the data hold time after            0x5
                                             SCL goes LOW
                                             Hold time = (2 * tpclk) + (2 + T_SP +
                                             T_HDDAT) * tpclk * (TPM + 1)
                –             15:14   –      Reserved                                            –
                T_SCLRatio    13      R/W    The LOW period of the generated SCL clock           0x1
                                             is defined by the combination of
                                             T_SCLRatio and T_SCLHi values.
                                             When T_SCLRatio = 0, the LOW period is
                                             equal to HIGH period. When T_SCLRatio =
                                             1, the LOW period is roughly two times of
                                             HIGH period.
                                             SCL LOW period = (2 * tpclk) + (2 + T_SP +
                                             T_SCLHi * ratio) * tpclk * (TPM + 1)
                                             1: ratio = 2
                                             0: ratio = 1
                                             This field is only valid when the controller is
                                             in the master mode.
                T_SCLHi       12:4    R/W    The HIGH period of generated SCL clock is           0x10
                                             defined by T_SCLHi.

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                    68(346)
  9 I2C                                                                            9.2 Register Definition



                Name            Bit    Type   Description                                         Reset
                                              SCL HIGH period = (2 * tpclk) + (2 + T_SP +
                                              T_SCLHi) * tpclk * (TPM + 1)
                                              The T_SCLHi value must be greater than
                                              T_SP and T_HDDAT values.
                                              This field is only valid when the controller is
                                              in the master mode.
                DMAEn           3      R/W    Enable the direct memory access mode                0x0
                                              data transfer.
                                              1: Enable
                                              0: Disable
                Master          2      R/W    Configure this device as a master or a slave        0x0
                                              1: Master mode
                                              0: Slave mode
                Addressing      1      R/W    I2C addressing mode:                                0x0
                                              1: 10-bit addressing mode
                                              0: 7-bit addressing mode
                IICEn           0      R/W    Enable the I2C controller                           0x0
                                              1: Enable
                                              0: Disable



               Timing Parameter Multiplier Register (0x30)
                    The Timing Parameter Multiplier register is used to store a multiplier
               value that amplifies the I2C bus timing parameters defined in the Setup
               register. This multiplier value helps the controller meet the timing
               requirements of the I2C bus interface when the controller is running at a
               higher APB clock frequency. For the value calculation, see 9.3.5 Timing
               Parameter Multiplier.
                   The definition of Timing Parameter Multiplier register is shown in Table
               9-11.
               Table 9-11 Timing Parameter Multiplier Register
                Name     Bit    Type   Description                                                Reset
                -        31:5   -      Reserved                                                   -
                TPM      4:0    R/W    A multiplication value for I2C timing parameters. All      0x0
                                       the timing parameters in the Setup Register are
                                       multiplied by (TPM + 1).



MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                     69(346)
  9 I2C                                                                               9.3 Timing Setting




9.3 Timing Setting
                      Requirements before enabling the I2C controller:
                     Establish Timing Parameter Multiplier by programming the Timing
                      Parameter Multiplier register.
                     Establish the I2C bus timing parameters by programming the Setup
                      register.
                   The following sections describe how to set up the Setup register to
               meet the timing parameters of the I2C bus. Assuming that the APB clock is
               40MHz, the APB clock cycle is 25ns. If the APB clock frequency of the
               user design is not 40MHz, please set up the registers as follows.

9.3.1 Peak Suppression Width

                    Table 9-12 describes the suppression of peak pulse widths through
               the input filter.
                   For fast and ultra-fast modes, peaks of less than 50ns must be
               suppressed (assuming TPM == 0).
                      T_SP = 50ns / (25ns * (TPM + 1)) = 2
               Table 9-12 Timing Parameters for Spike Suppression
                Symbol     Parameter                    Standard-   Fast-         Fast-mode       Unit
                                                        mode        mode          Plus
                                                        Min   Max   Min     Max   Min     Max
                tSP        Pulse width of spikes that   -     -     0       50    0       50      ns
                           must be suppressed by
                           the input filter.


9.3.2 Data Setup Time

                   The Data Setup Time defines the time that SDA should remain stable
               before the rising edge of SCL.
                      Table 9-13 describes the timing parameters for the data setup time.
                      Table 9-10 describes the data setup time equation as follows:
                      Setup time = (2 * tpclk) + (2 + T_SP + T_SUDAT) * tpclk * (TPM + 1)
                      For example, in standard mode (assuming TPM == 0),
                      250ns = 50ns + (2 + 2 + T_SUDAT) * 25ns
                      Calculated,

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                    70(346)
  9 I2C                                                                                     9.3 Timing Setting



                    T_SUDAT = 4
                    For other modes, T_SUDAT is calculated similarly.
               Table 9-13 Timing Parameters for the Data Setup Time
                Symbol   Parameter         Standard-mode         Fast-mode         Fast-mode Plus        Unit
                                           Min         Max       Min     Max       Min       Max
                tSUDAT   Data setup time   250         -         100     -         50        -           ns


9.3.3 Data Hold Time

                    The Data Hold Time defines the time that SDA should remain stable
               after the the falling edge of SCL.
                    Table 9-14 describes the timing parameters for the data hold time.
                    Table 9-10 describes the data hold time equation as follows:
                    Hold time = (2 * tpclk) + (2 + T_SP + T_HDDAT) * tpclk * (TPM + 1)
                    For example, in standard mode (assuming TPM == 0),
                    300ns = 50ns + (2 + 2 + T_HDDAT) * 25ns
                    Calculated,
                    T_HDDAT = 6
                    For other modes, T_HDDAT is calculated similarly.
               Table 9-14 Timing Parameters for the Data Hold Time
                Symbol   Parameter         Standard-mode         Fast-mode       Fast-mode Plus          Unit
                                           Min     Max           Min   Max       Min         Max
                tHDDAT   Data hold time    300     –             300   –         0           –           ns


9.3.4 I2C Bus Clock Frequency

                    The I2C bus clock frequency is defined by the parameters tHIGH and
               tLOW; Table 9-15 describes the T_SCLHi and T_SCL Ratio in the Setup
               registers that calculate the I2C bus clock frequency.
               Table 9-15 Timing Parameters for the SCL Clock
                Symbol   Parameter               Standard-         Fast-             Fast-mode           Unit
                                                 mode              mode              Plus
                                                 Min       Max     Min       Max     Min         Max
                tHIGH    HIGH period of the      4.0       -       0.6       -       0.26        -       us
                         SCL clock


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                           71(346)
  9 I2C                                                                         9.3 Timing Setting



                Symbol   Parameter            Standard-     Fast-         Fast-mode         Unit
                                              mode          mode          Plus
                                              Min   Max     Min     Max   Min      Max
                tLOW     LOW period of the    4.7   -       1.3     -     0.5               us
                         SCL clock



                   In standard mode, the minimum requirements of tHIGH and tLOW are
               close to each other, so T_SCLRatio can be set to 0 to simplify the
               calculation.Table 9-10 describes the SCL cycle equation as shown below:
                   SCL HIGH period = (2 * tpclk) + (2 + T_SP + T_SCLHi) * tpclk * (TPM +
               1) >= 4000ns
                  SCL LOW period = (2 * tpclk) + (2 + T_SP + T_SCLHi * ratio) * tpclk *
               (TPM + 1) >= 4700ns
                   Replacing T_SP in Equation 2, ratio = 1 and tpclk = 25ns, the equation
               simplifies to (assuming TPM == 0):
                   50ns + (2 + 2 + T_SCLHi) * 25ns * (0 + 1) >= 4000ns
                   50ns + (2 + 2 + T_SCLHi * 1) * 25ns * (0 + 1) >= 4700ns
                   T_SCLHi >= 182
                  In fast mode, the minimum requirement for tLOW is 2 times tHIGH, so
               T_SCLRatio can be set to 1. The SCL cycle equation is shown below:
                   SCL HIGH period = (2 * tpclk) + (2 + T_SP + T_SCLHi) * tpclk * (TPM +
               1) >= 600ns
                  SCL LOW period = (2 * tpclk) + (2 + T_SP + T_SCLHi * ratio) * tpclk *
               (TPM + 1) >= 1300ns
                   Replacing T_SP in Equation 2, ratio = 2 and tpclk = 25ns, the equation
               simplifies to (assuming TPM == 0):
                   50ns + (4 + T_SCLHi) * 25ns * (0 + 1) >= 600ns
                   50ns + (4 + T_SCLHi * 2) * 25ns * (0 + 1) >= 1300ns
                   T_SCLHi >= 23
                  In ultra fast mode, you can refer to the calculation method in the fast
               mode for T_SCLHi.

9.3.5 Timing Parameter Multiplier

                   To meet the timing requirements of the I2C bus, the TPM is calculated
               assuming that T_SCLHi is the maximum value (511) and T_SP is the least

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                              72(346)
  9 I2C                                                                      9.3 Timing Setting



               significant value (1).
                   For example, referring to Table 9-15 and Table 9-10, assume tSP =
               50ns, tHIGH = 4.0us, and tLOW = 4.7us.
                   SCL HIGH period = 2 * tpclk + (2 + T_SP + T_SCLHi) * tpclk * (TPM +
               1) >= 4.0us
                  SCL LOW period = 2 * tpclk + (2 + T_SP + T_SCLHi * ratio) * tpclk *
               (TPM + 1) >= 4.7us
                   It is known that T_SP = 1, T_SCLHi = 511, ratio = 1, and tpclk = 2ns.
                   SCL HIGH period = 2 * 2ns + (2 + 1 + 511) * 2ns * (TPM + 1) >= 4.0us
                   SCL LOW period = 2 * 2ns + (2 + 1 + 511 * 1) * 2ns * (TPM + 1) >=
               4.7us
                   Therefore,
                   2 * 2ns + (2 + 1 + 511) * 2ns * (TPM + 1) >= 4000ns
                   2 * 2ns + (2 + 1 + 511 * 1) * 2ns * (TPM + 1) >= 4700ns
                   So:


                   TPM >= 3.57
                   Calculated TPM = 4, which is used to calculate all the timing
               parameters shown in Table 9-10.
                   That is, calculate T_SP:
                   T_SP >= 50ns / ((TPM+1) * tpclk) = 50ns / ((4 + 1) * 2ns) = 5
                   Calculate T_SCLHi:
                   SCL HIGH period = 2 * tpclk + (2 + T_SP + T_SCLHi) * tpclk * (TPM +
               1) >= 4000ns
                  SCL LOW period = 2 * tpclk + (2 + T_SP + T_SCLHi * ratio) * tpclk *
               (TPM + 1) >= 4700ns
                   Therefore,
                   SCL HIGH period = 2 * 2ns + (2 + 5 + T_SCLHi) * 2ns * (4 + 1) >=
               4000ns
                   SCL LOW period = 2 * 2ns + (2 + 5 + T_SCLHi) * 2ns * (4 + 1) >=
               4700ns
                   Combine the two equations to obtain:
                   2 * 2ns + (2 + 5 + T_SCLHi) * 2ns * (4 + 1) >= 4700ns
                   7 + T_SCLHi >= (4700 – 4) / (2 * 5)
MUG1029-1.3E                                                                           73(346)
  9 I2C                                                                     9.4 Driver Function Definition



                    T_SCLHi >= 462.6
                    Therefore, T_SCLHi = 463, T_SP = 5, and TPM = 4.

  10 SPI                                                                  10.1 Introduction




                                                                  10              SPI

10.1 Introduction
                   Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC contains an SPI controller that supports
               both master and slave modes. If used as SPI master mode, the controller
               can connect multiple SPI slave devices; if used as SPI slave mode, the
               controller can respond to master requests for data exchange.

10.1.1 Features

                  Supports AMBA 3.0 APB bus protocal specification
                  Supports MSB/LSB priority transmission
                  Supports DMA transfer
                  Supports programmable SPI SCLK
                  Supports SPI slave mode
                  Supports Quad I/O SPI interface
                  Supports 128-bit TX/RX FIFO depth
                  Supports 32-bit TX/RX FIFO width

10.1.2 Block Diagram

                    Figure 10-1 shows the block diagram of SPI.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                       79(346)
  10 SPI                                                                         10.1 Introduction



               Figure 10-1 SPI Block Diagram


                                                         APB Bus




                                                        Register File




                                                        Control Logic




                                 SPI Arbiter




                                        SPI Interface



                                                                   HOL
                       SCLK      CS     MOSI     MISO      WP
                                                                    D

10.1.3 Functional Description

                  The SPI controller includes master mode, slave mode, and Quad I/O
               mode.
                    The SPI controller can act as an SPI master mode and initiate SPI
               transfer on the SPI bus. The SPI transfer format and interface timing can
               be programmed and configured using the APB programming port.
                    The SPI controller can act as an SPI slave mode and receive common
               commands as shown in Table 10-1. In addition, the controller supports
               user-defined commands, and the slave data field format is defined by the
               transfer control register.
               Table 10-1 Supported Commands under the Slave Mode
                Slave Command Name        OP Code                Slave Data
                Read Status Single IO     0x05                   32-bit Status
                Read Status Dual IO       0x15                   32-bit Status
                Read Status Quad IO       0x25                   32-bit Status


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                              80(346)
  10 SPI                                                                       10.2 Register Definition



                Slave Command Name           OP Code              Slave Data
                Read Data Single IO          0x0B                 Replay data from the Data
                                                                  Register in the FIFO manner
                Read Data Dual IO            0x0C                 Replay data from the Data
                                                                  Register in the FIFO manner
                Read Data Quad IO            0x0E                 Replay data from the Data
                                                                  Register in the FIFO manner
                Write Data Single IO         0x51                 Data saved to the Data Register
                                                                  in the FIFO manner
                Write Data Dual IO           0x52                 Data saved to the Data Register
                                                                  in the FIFO manner
                Write Data Quad IO           0x54                 Data saved to the Data Register
                                                                  in the FIFO manner
                User-defined                 Any 8-bit numbers    Depending on the Transfer
                                             other than the       Controller Register
                                             listed OP Codes



                    Quad I/O mode achieves four times the SPI bandwidth by combining
               the master input/slave output (MISO), master output/slave input (MOSI),
               write protection (WP), and HOLD signals into bidirectional signal lines.

               Table 10-4 SPI Transfer Format Register
                Name        Bit     Type   Description                                         Reset

                                           1: 2 bytes

                –           15:13   –      Reserved                                            –

                                           (DataLen + 1)
                DataMerge   7       R/W    Enable Data Merge mode, which does                  0x1
                                           automatic data split on write and data
                                           coalescing on read.
                                           This bit only takes effect when DataLen =
                                           0x7.
                                           Under Data Merge mode, each write to the
                                           Data Register will transmit all four bytes of
                                           the write data; each read from the Data
                                           Register will retrieve four bytes of received
                                           data as a single word data.
                                           When Data Merge mode is disabled, only the
                                           least (DataLen + 1) significant bits of the

                Name         Bit        Type    Description                                        Reset
                SlvDataOn    31         R/W     Data-only mode (slave mode only)                   0x0
                ly                              0: Disable the data-only mode
                                                1: Enable the data-only mode
                                                Note: This mode only works in the uni-
                                                directional regular (single) mode so
                                                MOSIiDir, DualQuad and TransMode
                                                should be set to 0
                CmdEn        30         R/W     SPI command phase enable (Master mode              0x0
                                                only)
                                                0: Disable the command phase
                                                1: Enable the command phase
                AddrEn       29         R/W     SPI address phase enable (Master mode              0x0
                                                only)
                                                0: Disable the address phase


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                        85(346)
  10 SPI                                                                    10.2 Register Definition



               Name        Bit     Type   Description                                      Reset
                                          1: Enable the address phase
               AddrFmt     28      R/W    SPI address phase format (Master mode            0x0
                                          only)
                                          0: Address phase is the regular (single)
                                          mode
                                          1: The format of the address phase is the
                                          same as the data phase (DualQuad)
               TransMode   27:24   R/W    Transfer mode                                    0x0
                                          The transfer sequence could be:
                                          0: Write and read at the same time
                                          1: Write only
                                          2: Read only
                                          3: Write, Read
                                          4: Read, Write
                                          5: Write, Dummy, Read
                                          6: Read, Dummy, Write
                                          7: None Data (must enable CmdEn or
                                          AddrEn in master mode)
                                          8: Dummy, Write
                                          9: Dummy, Read
                                          0xa~0xf: Reserved
               DualQuad    23:22   R/W    SPI data phase format                            0x0
                                          0: Regular (Single) mode
                                          1: Dual I/O mode
                                          2: Quad I/O mode
                                          3: Reserved
               TokenEn     21      R/W    Token transfer enable (Master mode only)         0x0
                                          Append a one-byte special token following
                                          the address phase for SPI read transfers.
                                          The value of the special token should be
                                          selected in TokenValue.
                                          0: Disable the one-byte special token
                                          1: Enable the one-byte special token
               WrTranCnt   20:12   R/W    Transfer count for write data
                                          WrTranCnt indicates the number of units of
                                          data to be transmitted to the SPI bus from
                                          the Data Register.


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                86(346)
  10 SPI                                                                   10.2 Register Definition



               Name        Bit    Type   Description                                      Reset
                                         The actual transfer count is (WrTranCnt +
                                         1).
                                         WrTranCnt only takes effect when
                                         TransMode is 0, 1, 3, 4, 5, 6 or 8.
                                         The size (bit-width) of a data unit is defined
                                         by the DataLen field of the Transfer Format
                                         Register.
                                         For TransMode 0, WrTranCnt must be
                                         equal to RdTranCnt.
               TokenValu   11     R/W    Token value (Master mode only)                   0x0
               e                         The value of the one-byte special token
                                         following the address phase for SPI read
                                         transfers.
                                         0: token value = 0x00
                                         1: token value = 0x69
               DummyCnt    10:9   R/W    Dummy data count. The actual dummy               0x0
                                         count is (DummyCnt + 1).
                                         The number of dummy cycles on the SPI
                                         interface will be (DummyCnt+1) *
                                         ((DataLen+1)/SPI IO width)
                                         The Data pins are put into the high
                                         impedance during the dummy data phase.
                                         DummyCnt is only used for TransMode 5,
                                         6, 8 and 9, which has dummy data phases.
                                         The =dummy cycle settings under some
                                         common transfer formats is as shown in
                                         Table 10-7.
               RdTranCnt   8:0    R/W    Transfer count for read data                     0x0
                                         RdTranCnt indicates the number of units of
                                         data to be received from SPI bus and
                                         stored to the Data Register. The actual
                                         received count is (RdTranCnt+1).
                                         RdTransCnt only takes effect when
                                         TransMode is 0, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 or 9.
                                         The size (bit-width) of a data unit is defined
                                         by the DataLen field of the Transfer Format
                                         Register.
                                         For TransMode 0, WrTranCnt must equal
                                         RdTranCnt.

               SPI Command Register (0x24)
                    A write operation in the SPI Command register triggers an SPI
               transfer. This register must be written to a null value to enable an SPI
               transfer even if it is not enabled from the command. When the SPI
               controller is in slave mode, the SPI Command register stores the
               command field of the last received SPI transaction.
                    The definition of SPI Command register is as shown in Table 10-8.
               Table 10-8 SPI Command Register
                Name          Bit          Type          Description                  Reset
                –             31:8         –             Reserved                     –
                CMD           7:0          R/W           SPI Command                  0x0



               SPI Address Register (0x28)
                    The definition of SPI Address register is as shown in Table 10-9.
               Table 10-9 SPI Address Register
                Name         Bit         Type         Description                         Reset
                ADDR         31:0        R/W          SPI Address                         0x0
                                                      (Master mode only)



               SPI Data Register (0x2C)
                    When the controller is in data merging mode, the byte storage order
               of the SPI Data register is in little-endian mode. The definition of SPI Data
               register is as shown in Table 10-10.


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                    88(346)
  10 SPI                                                                          10.2 Register Definition



               Table 10-10 SPI Data Register
                Name    Bit    Type    Description                                                Reset
                DATA    31:0   RW      Data to transmit or the received data                      0x0
                                       For writes, data is enqueued to the TX FIFO. The
                                       least significant byte is always transmitted first. If
                                       the TX FIFO is full and the SPIActive bit of the
                                       status register is 1, the ready signal hready/pready
                                       will be deasserted to insert wait states to the
                                       transfer.
                                       For reads, data is read and dequeued from the RX
                                       FIFO. The least significant byte is the first received
                                       byte. If the RX FIFO is empty and the SPIActive bit
                                       of the status register is 1, the ready signal
                                       hready/pready will be deasserted to insert wait
                                       states to the transfer.
                                       The FIFOs decouple the speed of the SPI transfers
                                       and the software’s generation/consumption of data.
                                       When the TX FIFO is empty, SPI transfers will hold
                                       until more data is written to the TX FIFO; when the
                                       RX FIFO is full, SPI transfers will hold until there is
                                       more room in the RX FIFO.
                                       If more data is written to the TX FIFO than the write
                                       transfer count (WrTranCnt), the remaining data will
                                       stay in the TX FIFO for the next transfer or until the
                                       TX FIFO is reset.



               SPI Control Register (0x30)
