               Table 10-11 SPI Control Register
                Name           Bit     Type    Description                                        Reset
                –              31:24   –       Reserved                                           –
                TXTHRES        23:16   R/W     Transmit (TX) FIFO Threshold                       0x0
                                               The TXFIFOInt interrupt or DMA request
                                               would be issued to replenish the TX FIFO
                                               when the TX data count is less than or
                                               equal to the TX FIFO threshold.
                RXTHRES        15:8    R/W     Receive (RX) FIFO Threshold                        0x0
                                               The RXFIFOInt interrupt or DMA request
                                               would be issued for consuming the RX

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                      89(346)
  10 SPI                                                                       10.2 Register Definition



                Name          Bit     Type   Description                                       Reset
                                             FIFO when the RX data count is more than
                                             or equal to the RX FIFO threshold.
                –             7:5     –      Reserved                                          –
                TXDMAEN       4       R/W    TX DMA enable                                     0x0
                RXDMAEN       3       R/W    RX DMA enable                                     0x0
                TXFIFORST     2       R/W    Transmit FIFO reset                               0x0
                                             Write 1 to reset. It is automatically cleared
                                             to 0 after the reset operation completes.
                RXFIFORST     1       R/W    Receive FIFO reset                                0x0
                                             Write 1 to reset. It is automatically cleared
                                             to 0 after the reset operation completes.
                SPIRST        0       R/W    SPI reset                                         0x0
                                             Write 1 to reset. It is automatically cleared
                                             to 0 after the reset operation completes.

  10 SPI                                                                            10.2 Register Definition



                Name          Bit         Type   Description                                        Reset
                                                 the SPI command register is written and
                                                 becomes 0 after the transfer is finished.
                                                 In slave mode, SPIActive becomes 1 after

               SPI Interface Timing Register (0x40)
                    The SPI Interface Timing register controls SPI interface timing to meet
               SPI slave interface timing requirements. This register needs to be
               programmed for master mode only. The definition of SPI Interface Timing
               register is as shown in Table 10-15.
               Table 10-15 SPI Interface Timing Register
                Name        Bit         Type       Description                            Reset
                –           31:14       –          Reserved                               –
                CS2SCLK     13:12       R/W        The minimum time between the           Configuration
                                                   edges of SPI CS and the edges of       dependent
                                                   SCLK.
                                                   The actual duration is
                                                   (SCLK period / 2) × (CS2SCLK + 1)
                CSHT        11:8        R/W        The minimum time that SPI CS           Configuration
                                                   should stay HIGH.                      dependent
                                                   The actual duration is
                                                   (SCLK period / 2) × (CSHT + 1)
                SCLK_DIV    7:0         R/W        The clock frequency ratio between      Configuration
                                                   the clock source and SPI interface     dependent
                                                   SCLK.
                                                   SCLK period = ((SCLK_DIV + 1) ×
                                                   2) × (Period of the SPI clock
                                                   source)
                                                   The SCLK_DIV value 0xff is a
                                                   special value which indicates that
                                                   the SCLK frequency should be the
                                                   same as the spi_clock frequency.

10.3 Driver Function Definition

10.3.1 Driver Function Definition

                    The definition of SPI driver function is shown in Table 10-22. The SPI
               driver function definition is located in bsp\driver\ae350\spi_ae350.c,
               spi_ae350.h and bsp\driver\include\Driver_SPI.h.
               Table 10-22 Driver Function Definition
                Driver Function      Description
                GetVersion           Gets the version information of the SPI drivers
                GetCapabilities      Gets the capability information of the SPI drivers
                Initialize           Initializes the SPI interface
                Uninitialize         Uninstalls the SPI Interface
                PowerControl         Specifies the power consumption mode of the SPI interface
                Send                 Transmits data via SPI-driven transmitter
                Receive              Receives data from SPI-driven receiver
                Transfer             Transfers data via SPI interface
                GetDataCount         Gets the data count transfered by the SPI interface
                Control              Configures the settings of the SPI interface to perform the
                                     specified operations
                GetStatus            Gets the status of the SPI interface




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                        98(346)
  10 SPI                                                                  10.3 Driver Function Definition

               detail.

               Table 10-23 Definition of GetVersion Function

                Return Value    Gets version information of the SPI drivers

               PowerControl
                   The definition of the PowerControl function is shown in Table 10-27.
               Table 10-27 Definition of PowerControl Function
                Prototype       int32_t (*PowerControl)(AE350_POWER_STATE state)
                Description     Specifies the power consumption mode of the SPI interface
                Parameter       state: SPI interface power consumption mode, including:
                                AE350_POWER_FULL: Set up peripherals for data transfers,
                                enable interrupts and DMA
                                AE350_POWER_LOW: Enable power-saving
                                AE350_POWER_OFF : Terminate pending data transfers and
                                disable peripherals, related interrupts and DMA
                Return Value    Returns a negative value if an execution error occurs

               Transfer

  11 RTC                                                                     11.1 Introduction




                                                                 11               RTC

11.1 Introduction
                  Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC contains a low-power Real Time Clock
               (RTC) to store the current time and provides periodic and alarm interrupts.

11.1.1 Features

                  Accesses registers through the AMBA 2.0 APB interface
                  Configurable counter size
                  Periodic interrupts: including half a second, a second, a minute, an
                   hour, and a day
                  Programmable alarm interrupts
                  Supports hardware digital trimming to compensate for the inaccuracy
                   of external clock sources

11.1.2 Block Diagram

                    Figure 11-1 shows the block diagram of RTC.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                        107(346)
  11 RTC                                                                  11.2 Register Definition



               Figure 11-1 RTC Block Diagram


                                 APB Clock            RTC Clock
                                  Domain               Domain              RTC Clock
                                                                           (32768 Hz)
                                  Pulse Generator                           Frequency Test


                                     1Hz Pulse                                 Alarm
                                                                              Wakeup
                                       Sync                 Time
                                                           Counter
                   APB Bus
                                    APB
                                  Interface
               Interfaces



11.2 Register Definition

11.2.1 Register Definition

                    The definition of RTC register is as shown in Table 11-1. RTC register
               definition is located in bsp\ae350\ae350.h.
               Table 11-1 Register Definition
                Address Offset           Name          Description
                0x00                     IDREV         ID and Revision Register
                0x04~0x0C                –             Reserved
                0x10                     CNTR          Counter Register
                0x14                     ALARM         Alarm Register
                0x18                     CTRL          Control Register
                0x1C                     STATUS        Status Register
                0x20                     TRIM          Digital Trimming Register


11.2.2 Register Description

                    The following sections describe the RTC register definitions in details.
                    The register type abbreviations are summarized as follows:
                   RO: Read-only
                   WO: Write-only
                   R/W: Readable and writable

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                            108(346)
  11 RTC                                                                        11.2 Register Definition



                    W1C: Write 1 to clear
               ID and Revision Register (0x00)
                    The ID and Revision registers are used to store ID and Revision
               numbers, with initial values dependent on the version used. The definition
               of ID and Revision registers are shown in Table 11-2.
               Table 11-2 ID and Revision Register
                Name       Bit     Type    Description                    Reset
                ID         31:8    RO      ID number for RTC              0x030110
                Major      7:4     RO      Major revision number          Revision Dependent
                Minor      3:0     RO      Minor revision number          Revision Dependent



               Counter Register (0x10)
                  The Counter register records the current time. The definition of
               Counter register is shown in Table 11-3.
               Table 11-3 Counter Register
                Name       Bit     Type    Description                                         Reset
                Day        31:17   R/W     Day passed after RTC enabled                        0x0
                Hour       16:12   R/W     Hour field of current time; range: 0~23             0x0
                Min        11:6    R/W     Minute field of current time; range: 0~23           0x0
                Sec        5:0     R/W     Second field of current time; range: 0~59           0x0



               Alarm Register (0x14)
                    When the value of Counter register matches the value of Alarm
               register, the alarm interrupt or alarm wakeup signal will be triggered.
                   The "Alarm_Int" and "Alarm_Wakeup" bits of the Control register
               control the alarm interrupt and alarm wakeup signal.
                      The definition of Alarm register is shown in Table 11-4.
               Table 11-4 Alarm Register
                Name      Bit      Type   Description                                          Reset
                Hour      16:12    R/W    Hour field of alarm setting; range: 0~23             0x0
                Min       11:6     R/W    Minute of field of alarm settings; range: 0~59       0x0
                Sec       5:0      R/W    Second field of alarm settings; range: 0~59          0x0




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                  109(346)
  11 RTC                                                                          11.2 Register Definition



               Control Register (0x18)
                    The Control register controls RTC enable/disable and interrupts. The
               definition of Control register is shown in Table 11-5.
               Table 11-5 Control Register
                Name              Bit   Type    Description                                       Reset
                Freq_Test_En      8     R/W     Enable the 512 Hz frequency test output           0x0
                Hsec              7     R/W     Enable half-second interrupt; half-second         0x0
                                                interrupt is generated when half a second
                                                passed
                Sec               6     R/W     Enable second interrupt; second interrupt is      0x0
                                                generated when one second passed
                Min               5     R/W     Enable minute interrupt; minute interrupt is      0x0
                                                generated when the seconds of RTC time
                                                changes from 59 to 0
                Hour              4     R/W     Enable hour interrupt; hour interrupt is          0x0
                                                generated when the minutes of RTC time
                                                changes from 59 to 0
                Day               3     R/W     Enable day interrupt; day interrupt is            0x0
                                                generated when the hours of RTC time
                                                changes from 23 to 0
                Alarm_Int         2     R/W     Enable alarm interrupt                            0x0
                Alarm_Wakeup      1     R/W     Enable alarm wakeup signal                        0x0
                RTC_En            0     R/W     Enable RTC                                        0x0



               Status Register (0x1C)
                    The Status register records the interrupt status and the updated
               status of the RTC register. The definition of Status register is shown in
               Table 11-6.
               Table 11-6 Interrupt Status Register
                Name        Bit       Type    Description                                         Reset
                WriteDone   16        RO      This bit indicates the synchronization progress     1
                                              of RTC register updates. This bit becomes zero
                                              when any of RTC control registers (the
                                              Counter, Alarm and Control registers) are
                                              updated. It returns to one when all prior
                                              updates to these three registers have been
                                              successfully synchronized to the RTC clock

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                    110(346)
  11 RTC                                                                             11.2 Register Definition



                Name        Bit     Type       Description                                           Reset
                                               domain. While an RTC register update is being
                                               synchronized to the RTC clock domain, a
                                               second update to the same register may be
                                               dropped.
                                               Each of the RTC registers is synchronized
                                               independently while their synchronization
                                               status is lumped into this single bit. Thus writes
                                               to different RTC registers can be done in a
                                               batch before checking this bit.
                                               Since the frequency of the RTC clock is quite
                                               slow when compared to the typical frequency
                                               of the APB clock, the synchronization period
                                               can be pretty long. The APB clock domain
                                               should not be shut down while the
                                               synchronization is still in progress.
                –           15:8    -          Reserved                                              –
                Hsec        7       W1C        Half-second interrupt status; write 1 to clear        0x0
                Sec         6       W1C        Second interrupt status                               0x0
                Min         5       W1C        Minute interrupt status                               0x0
                Hour        4       W1C        Hour interrupt status                                 0x0
                Day         3       W1C        Day interrupt status                                  0x0
                Alarm_Int   2       W1C        Alarm interrupt status                                0x0



               Digital Trimming Register (0x20)
                    The Digital Trimming register stores the trimming coefficient and
               direction for each RTC time period. You can continue to program this
               register when the RTC is disabled. The definition of Digital Trimming
               register is shown in Table 11-7.
               Table 11-7 Digital Trimming Register
                Name        Bit         Type    Description                                          Reset
                Day_Sign    31          R/W     Sign bit for the trimming value for the second       0x0
                                                on the day boundary:
                                                1: Slow down the timer.
                                                0: Speed up the timer.
                -           30:29       -       Reserved                                             -
                Day_Trim    28:24       R/W     Digital trimming value for the second on the         0x0


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                        111(346)
  11 RTC                                                                     11.3 Driver Function Definition



                Name           Bit      Type   Description                                          Reset
                                               day boundary.
                Hour_Sign      23       R/W    Sign bit for the trimming value for the second       0x0
                                               on the hour boundary:
                                               1: Slow down the timer.
                                               0: Speed up the timer.
                -              22:21    -      Reserved                                             -
                Hour_Trim      20:16    R/W    Digital trimming value for the second on the         0x0
                                               hour boundary.
                Min_Sign       15       R/W    Sign bit for the trimming value for the second       0x0
                                               on the minute boundary:
                                               1: Slow down the timer.
                                               0: Speed up the timer.
                -              14:13    -      Reserved                                             -
                Min_Trim       12:8     R/W    Digital trimming value for the second on the         0x0
                                               minute boundary.
                Sec_Sign       7        R/W    Sign bit for the trimming value for the rest of      0x0
                                               seconds:
                                               1: Slow down the timer.
                                               0: Speed up the timer.
                -              6:5      -      Reserved                                             -
                Sec_Trim       4:0      R/W    Digital trimming value for the rest of seconds       0x0

  12 PIT                                                                     12.1 Introduction




                                                                   12                PIT

12.1 Introduction
                   Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC contains a Programmable Interval Timer
               (PIT), which is a small multi-function timer that can be used as a PWM and
               simple timer.

12.1.1 Features

                  Supports AMBA 2.0 APB bus protocol specification
                  Supports up to 4 multi-function timers
                  Each multi-function timer can offer 6 applications (combinations of
                   timer and PWM)
                  Programmable timer clock source
                  Timers can be externally controlled to pause

12.1.2 Block Diagram

                   Figure 12-1 shows the block diagram of PIT.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                         117(346)
  12 PIT                                                                                   12.1 Introduction



                   Figure 12-1 PIT Block Diagram

                                        External clock



                                                                  PIT Channel 0       Channel 0 PWM
                        APB
                        Bus
                                         Register File




                                                                      ...
                                                                  PIT Channel n       Channel n PWM


                                                                             or       Timer interrupt



12.1.3 Functional Description

                       The PIT supports up to 4 PIT channels, and each PIT channel is a
                   multi-function timer that can provide 6 applications:
                       One 32-bit timer
                       Two 16-bit timers
                       Four 8-bit timers
                       One 16-bit PWM
                       One 16-bit timer and one 8-bit PWM
                       Two 8-bit timers and one 8-bit PWM
                         The PIT channel modes are shown in Table 12-1.
                   Table 12-1 Effective Devices of Channel Modes
  Channel      32-bit         16-bit       8-bit         PWM         Mixed PWM/16-     Mixed PWM/8-bit
  Mode         Timer          Timers       Timers                    bit Timer         Timers
  –            32-bit         16-bit       8-bit         –           16-bit Timer 0    8-bit Timer 0
               Timer 0        Timer 0      Timer 0
  –            –              16-bit       8-bit         –           –                 8-bit Timer 1
                              Timer 1      Timer 1
  –            –              –            8-bit         –           –                 –
                                           Timer 2
  –            –              –            8-bit         –           –                 –
                                           Timer 3
  –            –              –                          16-bit      8-bit PWM         8-bit PWM
                                                         PWM

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                       118(346)
  12 PIT                                                                   12.2 Register Definition

                    The definition of PIT register is as shown in Table 12-2. PIT register
               definition is located in bsp\ae350\ae350.h.
               Table 12-2 Register Definition
                Address Offset         Name            Description
                0x00                   IDREV           ID and Revision Register
                0x04~0x0C              –               Reserved
                0x10                   CFG             Configuration Register
                0x14                   INTEN           Interrupt Enable Register
                0x18                   INTST           Interrupt Status Register
                0x1C                   CHNEN           Channel Enable Register

                0x38                   Ch1Cntr         Channel 1 Counter Register

                                             disabled, this bit reflects the output of PWM and
                                             writing to it will change the output of PWM. The
                                             value of this bit also governs how the PWM
                                             waveform is generated when this channel is
                                             enabled.
                                             0: the PWM output is LOW when the channel is
                                             disabled; the low-period PWM counter will be

               Channel n Reload Register (0x24 + n * 0x10)
                   The Reload register is used to store the counter initial/reload value of
               PWM/Timer, which is dependent on the channel mode. The period of the
               PWM/Timer is equal to the value of Reload plus 1. For example, for 32-bit
               timer mode, a timing interrupt is generated every (TMR32_0 + 1) period.
               For PWM mode, the maximum period is (PWM16_Hi + 1) and the
               minimum period is (PWM16_Lo + 1).
                   Table 12-9, Table 12-10, Table 12-11, Table 12-12, Table 12-13, and
               Table 12-14 describe the definition of the Reload register.
               Table 12-9 Reload Register for 32-bit Timer Mode (ChMode = 1)

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                     132(346)
  13 WDT                                                                    13.1 Introduction




                                                              13               WDT

13.1 Introduction
                   Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC includes a WDT (Watchdog Timer) to
               prevent system lock-ups in case of program execution errors.

13.1.1 Features

                  Supports AMBA 2.0 APB bus protocol specification
                  If the WDT times out, it supports both interrupt and reset mechanisms
                  Supports a control/restart register write protection mechanism
                  Programmable timer clock source
                  Configurable magic numbers for register write protection and timer
                   restart
                  Supports external pause for WDT

13.1.2 Block Diagram

                   Figure 13-1 shows the block diagram of WDT.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                        133(346)
  13 WDT                                                                              13.2 Register Definition



               Figure 13-1 WDT Block Diagram



                                                                                             wdt_int
                                                                                             wdt_rst
               APB
                            Register File          WDT control
               Bus
                                                                                            pclk



                                                                                             extclk
                                                 Counter              Clock control          wdt_paus
                                                 enable                                          e



13.1.3 Functional Description

                     The WDT provides a two-stage mechanism to prevent system lockup.
                    The first one is called the interrupt stage. If the WDT interrupt is
               enabled and the WDT is not restarted during the interrupt stage, the
               interrupt signal "wdt_int" will be asserted.
                    The second one is called the reset stage and starts immediately after
               the interrupt stage. If the WDT reset is enabled and the WDT is not
               restarted during the interrupt stage, the reset signal "wdt_rst" will be
               asserted.

13.2 Register Definition

13.2.1 Register Definition

                    The definition of WDT register is as shown in Table 13-1. WDT
               register definition is located in bsp\ae350\ae350.h.
               Table 13-1 Register Definition
                Address Offset              Name                 Description
                0x00                        IDREV                ID and Revision Register
                0x04~0x0C                   –                    Reserved
                0x10                        CTRL                 Control Register
                0x14                        RESTART              Restart Register
                0x18                        WREN                 Write Enable Register
                0x1C                        ST                   Status Register



MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                        134(346)
  13 WDT                                                                           13.2 Register Definition




13.2.2 Register Description

                     The following sections describe the WDT register definitions in details.
                     The register type abbreviations are summarized as follows:
                    RO: Read-only
                    WO: Write-only
                    R/W1C: Readable and Write 1 to clear
                    WP: Write protected
                    R/WP: Readable and Write protected
                    DC: Don’t care
               ID and Revision Register (0x00)
                    The ID and Revision registers are used to store ID and Revision
               numbers, and the initial values are dependent on the version used. The
               definition of ID and Revision registers are shown in Table 13-2.
               Table 13-2 ID and Revision Register
                Name         Bit         Type    Description                   Reset
                ID           31:12       RO      ID number for WDT             0x03002
                RevMajor     11:4        RO      Major revision number         Revision Dependent
                RevMinor     3:0         RO      Minor revision number         Revision Dependent



               Control Register (0x10)
                   The Control register is write-protected to prevent accidental
               overwrites. The Write Enable register must be written before this register
               can be written. The definition of Control register is shown in Table 13-3.
               Table 13-3 Control Register
                Name       Bit       Type       Description                                       Reset
                -          31:11     -          Reserved                                          -
                RstTime    10:8      R/WP       The timer interval of the reset stage:            0x0
                                                0: Clock period × 27
                                                1: Clock period × 28
                                                2: Clock period × 29
                                                3: Clock period × 210
                                                4: Clock period × 211
                                                5: Clock period × 212

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                     135(346)
  13 WDT                                                                     13.2 Register Definition



                Name      Bit     Type    Description                                       Reset
                                                                13
                                          6: Clock period × 2
                                          7: Clock period × 214
                IntTime   7:4     R/WP    The timer interval of the interrupt stage:        0x0
                                                                6
                                          0: Clock period × 2
                                          1: Clock period × 28
                                          2: Clock period × 210
                                          3: Clock period × 211
                                          4: Clock period × 212
                                          5: Clock period × 213
                                          6: Clock period × 214
                                          7: Clock period × 215
                                          8: Clock period × 217
                                          9: Clock period × 219
                                          10: Clock period × 221
                                          11: Clock period × 223
                                          12: Clock period × 225
                                          13: Clock period × 227
                                          14: Clock period × 229
                                          15: Clock period × 231
                RstEn     3       R/WP    Enable or disable the watchdog reset              0x0
                                          0: Disable
                                          1: Enable
                IntEn     2       R/WP    Enable or disable the watchdog interrupt          0x0
                                          0: Disable
                                          1: Enable
                ClkSel    1       R/WP    Clock source of timer                             0x0
                                          0: EXTCLK
                                          1: PCLK
                En        0       R/WP    Enable or disable the watchdog timer              0x0
                                          0: Disable
                                          1: Enable



               Restart Register (0x14)
                    The Restart register is write-protected to prevent accidental overrides.
               The Write Enable register must be written before this register can be
               written.
MUG1029-1.3E                                                                               136(346)
  13 WDT                                                                                  13.2 Register Definition



                   If the Restart register is written with the pre-configured value
               "WDT_RESTART_NUM", it restarts the interrupt timer and terminates the
               system reset timer. If the pre-configured value is not
               "WDT_RESTART_NUM", the WDT ignores the write operation."
                    The definition of Restart register is shown in Table 13-4.
               Table 13-4 Restart Register
                Name       Bit             Type     Description                                           Reset
                -          31:16           -        Reserved                                              -
                Restart    15:0            WP       Write the magic number “WDT_RESTART_NUM”              DC
                                                    to restart the watchdog timer.



               Write Enable Register (0x18)
                    Both the Control and Restart registers can be programmed with the
               two-stage write mechanism, and the Write Enable register is programmed
               with a magic number "WDT_WREN_NUM" to disable the write protection
               mechanism before updating either register, and then enable the write
               protection mechanism of the registers again when any subsequent write
               operation to the WDT registers is received.
                    The definition of Write Enable register is shown in Table 13-5.
               Table 13-5 Write Enable Register
                Name      Bit          Type        Description                                            Reset
                –         31:16        –           Reserved                                               –
                WEn       15:0         WO          Write the magic number                                 DC
                                                   “WDT_WREN_NUM” to disable the write
                                                   protection of the Control Register and the Restart
                                                   Register.



               Status Register (0x1C)
                    The definition of Status register is shown in Table 13-6.
               Table 13-6 Status Register
                Name             Bit           Type      Description                                      Reset
                –                31:1          –         Reserved                                         –
                IntExpired       0             R/W1C     The status of the watchdog interrupt timer       0x0
                                                         0: timer is not expired ye
                                                         1: timer is expired

  14 DMA                                                                     14.1 Introduction




                                                                14               DMA

14.1 Introduction
                   Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC contains a Direct Memory Access (DMA)
               controller for efficient data transfer between devices on the bus.

14.1.1 Features

                  Supports AMBA AXI4 and APB4 bus protocol specifications
                  Supports up to 8 configurable DMA channels
                  Supports up to 16 sets of DMA requests/responses for hardware
                   handshaking
                  Supports up to 2 AXI master ports for data transfer
                  Supports up to 2 configurable DMA cores
                  Supports one APB slave port for DMA register programming
                  Configurable AXI address width: 24-64 bits
                  Configurable AXI data width: 32, 64, 128, or 256 bits
                  Supports a two-level priority polling arbitration mechanism
                  Supports DMA chain transfer

14.1.2 Block Diagram

                   Figure 14-1 shows the block diagram of DMA.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                        142(346)
  14 DMA                                                                   14.1 Introduction



               Figure 14-1 DMA Block Diagram




                         AXI Master 0                   AXI Master 1



                         dma_core_0                     dma_core_1

                           FIFO_0                         FIFO_1

                        DMA_engine_0                   DMA_engine_1




                         Register File                  Channel Mux



                          APB Slave                       Arbiter




14.1.3 Functional Description

                    The DMA controller includes two AXI master interfaces for data
               transfer and one APB slave interface for register programming.
                    The DMA controller supports up to 8 DMA channels, and each DMA
               channel provides a set of registers to describe the expected data
               transfers. While multiple DMA channels can be enabled simultaneously,
               each DMA core can control only one DMA channel at a time.
                   The DMA controller supports up to 16 sets of hardware handshake
               signals (dma_req/dma_ack) for data transfer for low-speed devices.
                    The DMA controller supports chain transfer, allowing continuous
               transfer of multiple discontinuous data blocks without the need for
               intervention by the main processor.
                   The DMA controller supports three address control modes: Increment
               mode, Decrement mode, and Fixed mode. In Increment mode, the DMA
               controller increments the address after accessing the source/destination

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                      143(346)
  14 DMA                                                         14.2 DMA Device Configuration



               data. In Decrement mode, the DMA controller decrements the address
               after accessing the source/destination data. In Fixed mode, the DMA
               controller keeps the address unchanged after accessing the
               source/destination data.

14.2 DMA Device Configuration

14.2.1 DMA Device

                   For all devices that support DMA, the hardware handshake IDs as
               shown in Table 14-1.
               Table 14-1 DMA Hardware Handshake ID
                Handshake ID              Devices
                2                         SPI TX
                3                         SPI RX
                4                         UART1 TX
                5                         UART1 RX
                6                         UART2 TX
                7                         UART2 RX
                8                         I2C

                                               DMA core and disable all channels.



               Channel Abort Register (0x24)
                    The Channel Abort register is used to control the termination of DMA
               channel transfer, and each channel is controlled using one bit. Transfers
               for pending channel n are terminated by writing 1 to bit n. Currently
               running transfers will still continue to complete to avoid any violation of the
               AXI protocol. The termination bit is cleared by writing 1 to the
               corresponding bit in the Interrupt Status register.
                    The definition of Channel Abort register is shown in Table 14-7.
               Table 14-7 Channel Abort Register
                Name          Bit       Type    Description                                                Reset
                ChAbort       N-        WO      Write 1 to bit n to abort channel n. The bits should       0x0


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                              148(346)
  14 DMA                                                                          14.3 Register Definition



                Name       Bit   Type    Description                                              Reset
                           1:0           only be set when the corresponding channels are
                                         enabled. Otherwise, the writes will be ignored for
                                         channels that are not enabled. (N: Number of
                                         channels)



               Interrupt Status Register (0x30)
                    Interrupt Status register includes terminal counter, error and
               termination status. When a channel encounters a terminal count event, the
               terminal count status for that channel is set. When a channel encounters
               an error/termination event, the error/termination status for that channel is
               set. Each channel has a status bit, which is set to 0 if the corresponding
               channel is not configured.
                     The Interrupt Status register definition is shown in Table 14-8.
               Table 14-8 Interrupt Status Register
                Name     Bit     Type      Description                                            Reset
                –        31:24   –         Reserved                                               –
                TC       23:16   R/W1C     The terminal count status, one bit per channel.        0x0
                                           The terminal count status is set when a channel
                                           transfer finishes without the abort or error event.
                                           0: Channel n has no terminal count status
                                           1: Channel n has terminal count status
                Abort    15:8    R/W1C     The abort status of channel, one bit per               0x0
                                           channel. The abort status is set when a channel
                                           transfer is aborted.
                                           0: Channel n has no abort status
                                           1: Channel n has abort status
                Error    7:0     R/W1C     The error status, one bit per channel. The error       0x0
                                           status is set when a channel transfer
                                           encounters the following error events:
                                           Bus error
                                           Unaligned address
                                           Unaligned transfer width
                                           Reserved configuration
                                           0: Channel n has no error status
                                           1: Channel n has error status




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                     149(346)

               Table 14-15 Channel N Destination Address High Part Register
                Name       Bit    Type   Description                                          Reset
                DstAddrH   31:0   R/W    High part of the destination starting address.       0x0
                                         When the transfer completes, the value of
                                         {DstAddrH, DstAddrL} is updated to the ending
                                         address.
                                         This address must be aligned to the destination
                                         transfer size; otherwise the error event will be
                                         triggered.
                                         This register exists only when the address bus
                                         width is wider than 32 bits.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                154(346)

                                                  descriptor. The pointer must be double word
                                                  aligned.
                -                2:1     -        Reserved                                            -
                LLDBusInfIdx     0       R/W      Bus interface index that the next descriptor        0x0
                                                  is read from
                                                  0: The next descriptor is read from bus
                                                  interface 0

                                                  interface 1

  15 PLMT                                                                      15.1 Introduction




                                                                  15            PLMT

15.1 Introduction
                    The RISC-V processor defines a Platform Level Machine Timer
               (PLMT) that provides a real-time counter as well as generating timer
               interrupts.

15.1.1 Block Diagram

                   Figure 15-1 shows the block diagram of PLMT.
               Figure 15-1 PLMT Block Diagram

                 mtime_clk                               stoptime
                                      mtime
                                                           mtip        A25

                                    mtimecmp




                                    AHB/AXI


15.1.2 Functional Description

                    PLMT is different from the traditional RTC. According to the RISC-V
               Privileged Specification, the timer clock (mtime_clk) can work at any
               frequency, as long as it is a fixed-frequency clock, and it is not affected by
               clock gating or frequency scaling of other clocks on the platform, while
               RTC typically uses a 32768Hz clock. In most cases, the timer clock can
               share the same clock source as the RTC clock. The RISC-V Privileged
               Specification requires the software to obtain the frequency of the timer
               clock through platform-specific mechanisms.

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                          159(346)
  15 PLMT                                                               15.2 Register Definition



                   PLMT mainly consists of the following memory-mapped registers:
               mtime and mtimecmp. The mtime register is a 64-bit real-time counter
               clocked by mtime_clk.
                   The mtimecmpn register stores a 64-bit value for comparison with
               mtime. When the value of mtime is greater than or equal to the value of
               mtimecmpn, the mtip[n] signal is asserted, generating a timer interrupt.
               When mtimecmpn is written to, the interrupt is cleared, and the mtip[n]
               signal is asserted.

15.2 Register Definition

15.2.1 Register Definition

                    The definition of PLMT register is as shown in Table 15-1. PLMT
               register definition is located in bsp\ae350\ae350.h.
               Table 15-1 Register Definition
                Address Offset       Name               Description
                0x00~0x03            MTIME[0]           mtime[31:0]
                0x04~0x07            MTIME[1]           mtime[63:32]
                0x08~0x0B            MTIMECMP0[0]       mtimecmp0[31:0] for hart 0
                0x0C~0x0F            MTIMECMP0[1]       mtimecmp0[63:32] for hart 0
                0x10~0x13            MTIMECMP1[0]       mtimecmp1[31:0] for hart 1
                0x14~0x17            MTIMECMP1[1]       mtimecmp1[63:32] for hart 1
                0x18~0x1B            MTIMECMP2[0]       mtimecmp2[31:0] for hart 2
                0x1C~0x1F            MTIMECMP2[1]       mtimecmp2[63:32] for hart 2
                0x20~0x23            MTIMECMP3[0]       mtimecmp3[31:0] for hart 3
                0x24~0x27            MTIMECMP3[1]       mtimecmp3[63:32] for hart 3


15.2.2 Register Description

                   The following sections describe the PLMT register definitions in
               details.
                   The register type abbreviations are summarized as follows:
                  R/W: Readable and Writeable
               Machine Time Register (0x00~0x04)
                   The definition of Machine Time register is shown in Table 15-2.


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                          160(346)
  15 PLMT                                                                       15.2 Register Definition



               Table 15-2 Machine Time Register
                Name    Bit     Type   Description                                              Reset
                mtime   63:0    R/W    A constant frequency real-time counter. A timer          0x0
                                       interrupt is generated when
                                       mtime >= mtimecmp



               Machine Time Compare Register (0x08~0x0C)
                   The definition of Machine Time Compare register is shown in Table
               15-3.
               Table 15-3 Machine Time Compare Register
                Name          Bit    Type   Description                      Reset
                mtimecmp      63:0   R/W    A timer interrupt is generated   0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF
                                            when
                                            mtime >= mtimecmp
                                            The timer interrupt input is
                                            de-asserted when this
                                            register is written.

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                         171(346)
  17 Flash                                                                   17.1 Introduction




                                                               17                 Flash

17.1 Introduction
                    Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC includes a default Instruction Memory,
               which can be configured as SPI-Flash Memory. This memory serves the
               purpose of storing and executing instructions. Additionally, it can be
               connected to the Extended APB Slave bus interface to function as a
               peripheral for Flash operations such as read, write, and erase.
                  The SPI-Flash Memory consists of an SPI controller, and you can see
               Chapter 10 SPI for more details.

17.2 Command Definition
                   SPI-Flash Memory command definitions are as shown in Table 17-1.
               Table 17-1 Command Definition
                Command                              Value   Description
                AE350_FLASH_WRITE_CMD                0x01    Write data
                AE350_FLASH_PROGRAM_CMD              0x02    Page program
                AE350_FLASH_READ_CMD                 0x03    Read data
                AE350_FLASH_WRITE_DISABLE_CMD        0x04    Write enable
                AE350_FLASH_READ_STATUS_CMD          0x05    Read status
                AE350_FLASH_WRITE_ENABLE_CMD         0x06    Write enable
                AE350_FLASH_ERASE_4K_CMD             0x20    Sector erase (4KB)
                AE350_FLASH_ERASE_64K_CMD            0xD8    Block erase (64KB)
                AE350_FLASH_ERASE_CHIP_CMD           0x60    Chip erase




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                        172(346)
  17 Flash                                                                    17.3 Register Definition




17.3 Register Definition
                   For the definition of SPI-Flash Memory register, you can see Section
               10.2 Register Definition in Chapter 10 SPI for more details.

17.4 Driver Function Definition

17.4.1 Driver Function Definition

                   The definition of SPI-Flash Memory driver is as shown in Table 17-2.
               The SPI-Flash Memory driver function definition is located in
               extp\flash\flash.c and flash.h.
               Table 17-2 Driver Function Definition
                Driver Function      Description
                Initialize           Initializes SPI-Flash Memory
                SwitchMode           Switches between SPI-Flash Memory programming mode
                                     and memory read/write mode
                Read                 Read data
                Write                Write data
                Program              Write data in page
                Erase4KSec           Erase data in sector (4KB)
                Erase64KSec          Erase data in block ( 64KB)

                    The following sections describe the SPI-Flash Memory driver function
               definitions in detail.
               Initialize
                    The definition of the Initialize function is shown in Table 17-3.
               Table 17-3 Definition of Initialize Function

                Description       Initializes SPI-Flash Memory

                Return Value      Returns a negative value if an execution error occurs




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                173(346)

               SwitchMode
                   The definition of the SwitchMode function is shown in Table 17-4.

  18 Ethernet                                                                  18.1 Introduction




                                                           18              Ethernet

18.1 Introduction
                     Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC includes an Extended AHB Slave bus
                interface, which can be interconnected with the Gowin Triple Speed
                Ethernet MAC IP, enabling the implementation of an Ethernet peripheral.
                     Gowin Triple Speed Ethernet MAC IP implements the functions
                described in the IEEE802.3 protocol for the MAC layer. It can be
                integrated into devices requiring Ethernet MAC for communication
                applications.

18.1.1 Feature

                   Supports AMBA 3.0 AHB bus specification
                   Supports MII/GMII/RGMII interfaces
                   Complies with the IEEE802.3 standard
                   Supports 10/100/1000M data rate
                   Supports full duplex and half duplex, and collision detection in half
                    duplex mode
                   Supports user-selectable automatic addition and verification of CRC
                   Supports automatic addition of pad
                   Supports Ethernet frame classification statistics
                   Supports Ethernet frame error statistics
                   Supports IFG configuration
                   Supports Jumbo mode
                   Supports Flow Control in full duplex mode


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                          176(346)
  18 Ethernet                                                               18.1 Introduction



                   Supports MDC and MDIO interfaces

18.1.2 Operating Frequency

                   The Ethernet operating frequency depends on the current operation
                mode as follows:
                   When IP is configured to RGMII mode, the operating frequency
                    supports 1000/100/10MHz.
                   When IP is configured to GMII mode, the operating frequency supports
                    1000MHz.
                   When IP is configured to MII mode, the operating frequency supports
                    100/10MHz.
                   When IP is configured to GMII/MII mode, the operating frequency
                    supports 1000/100/10MHz.

18.1.3 Block Diagram

                    The structure diagram of Ethernet is as shown in Figure 18-1.
                Figure 18-1 Structure Diagram of Ethernet


                                             AHB Bus

                      Ethernet
                                            Register File


                           MAC_TX
                                                   MAC_IF
                           MAC_RX


                         Management



18.1.4 Functional Description

                   Ethernet mainly contains modules such as MAC_IF, MAC_RX,
                MAC_TX, Management, Register File.


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                        177(346)
  18 Ethernet                                                              18.2 Register Definition



                    MAC_TX module realizes the conversion from user data format to
                Ethernet data format, as well as CRC, PAD, Flow Control, frame statistics
                and other functions.
                     MAC_RX module realizes the conversion from Ethernet data format to
                user data format, as well as CRC, Flow Control, IFG configuration, frame
                statistics, error indication and other functions.
                     MAC_IF module realizes the function of Ethernet data adaption to
                various MAC interfaces, such as RGMII, GMII, MII.
                   Management realizes MDC and MDIO functions of Ethernet
                Management interface and facilitates users to configure PHY IC.
                    The Register File defines registers for interacting with Gowin
                RiscV_AE350_SOC.

  19 CAN                                                                      19.1 Introduction




                                                                 19               CAN

19.1 Introduction
                    Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC includes an Extended AHB Slave bus
               interface, which can be interconnected with Gowin CAN, enabling the
               implementation of CAN peripheral.
                    CAN implements CAN 2.0A, CAN 2.0B, and the updated high-
               performance non-ISO CAN-FD protocol. It can be integrated into devices
               that require CAN, commonly used in automotive and industrial
               applications.

19.1.1 Feature

                  Supports AMBA 3.0 AHB bus specification
                  Compliant with CAN2.0A and CAN2.0B protocols and ISO 11898-1
                   standard
                  Supports CAN-FD
                   -   Non-ISO CAN FD
                   -   Compliant with Bosch protocol
                   -   Compliant with ISO/DIS 11898-1
                  Supports independent system clock and CAN bus clock
                   -   The system clock consists of the CPU interface bus clock and the
                       IP system clock, which must be a continuous clock and cannot be
                       interrupted.
                   -   The CAN bus clock can be independent or connected to the
                       system clock.
                  Flexible shared buffer solution to achieve optimal buffer size to store,

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                         189(346)
  19 CAN                                                                             19.1 Introduction



                     send, and receive messages in given applications
                    Total buffer depth can be configured via parameters
                     -      Transmit buffer, receive buffer, and high-priority transmit buffer.
                     -      The CPU can independently configure the depth of each buffer for
                            transmit, receive, and high-priority transmit buffer operations.
                    Receive filters can be configured: 1 to 16
                    Programmable baud rate prescaler

19.1.2 Block Diagram

                         Figure 19-1 shows the block diagram of CAN.
               Figure 19-1 CAN Block Diagram



                                         TRANSMIT
                                            HPB
                                                           TX
                                                                        BIT STREAM
                                                       SCHEDULER
                                         TRANSMIT                       PROCESSING
                                            LPB                                      txd

                   SYSCLK                                                                   CAN
                                                            RX                              XVER
                                        RCV BUFFER     ACCEPTANCE                    rxd
                                                         FILTERS
                               CPU                                      BIT TIMING
                                I/F




                   Interrupt               CSR
                                                                       TQCLK

                                                                           BRP
                                         Interrupt

                                         Control                         Clock Gen

                                                                      CANCLK




19.1.3 Functional Description

                   Bit Timing Logic (BTL) is used to achieve bit synchronization of the
               controller and CAN communication using synchronization segments,
               propagation segments, and phase segments.
                   Bit Stream Processor (BSP) is used to perform various higher-level
               protocol functions, typically categorized as MAC functions, including
               checking and removing CRC bits and padding bits in the receive direction.

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                190(346)
  19 CAN                                                                      19.2 Register Definition



                   The Bit Rate Prescaler (BRP) is used to generate the Time Quantum
               (TQ) by dividing the input CANCLK by the programmed prescaler value
               (BRP).
                     The RX receive filter filters input frames by comparing the 11-bit or 29-
               bit identifier in each message with the values in the RX filter registers
               (AFR) and the corresponding RX filter mask (AFM).
                   The TX scheduler implements strict Priority (SP) scheduling between
               TB and THPB messages, where messages in THPB have higher priority.
                   Configuration and Status Registers (CSR) implement various
               command, control, and status registers for operating the controller and
               conveying status to the CPU.

19.2 Register Definition

19.2.1 Register Definition

                    The definition of CAN register is as shown in Table 19-1. And CAN
               register definition is located in extp\can\can.h.
               Table 19-1 Register Definition
                Address Offset         Name          Description
                0x0000                 SRST          Software Reset Register
                0x0004                 CMD           Command Register
                0x0008                 BRP           Baud Rate Prescalar Register
                0x000C                 BTN           Bit Timing (Norminal) Register
                0x0010                 BTD           Bit Timing (Data) Register
                0x001C                 –             Reserved
                0x0020                 IS            Interrupt Status Register
                0x0024                 IE            Interrupt Enable Register
                0x0028                 IC            Interrupt Clear Register
                0x002C                 –             Reserved
                0x0030                 CFG           Configuration Register
                0x0034~0x003C          –             Reserved
                0x0040                 RXBCFG        Receive Buffer/FIFO Configuration
                                                     Register
                0x0044                 TXBCFG        Transmit Buffer/FIFO Configuration
                                                     Register


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                191(346)
  19 CAN                                                                      19.2 Register Definition



                Address Offset        Name            Description
                0x0048                TXHBCFG         Transmit High-priority Buffer/FIFO
                                                      Configuration Register
                0x004C                –               Reserved
                0x0050                TXBRETRY        Transmit Buffer Retry Counter Register
                0x0054                TXHBRETRY       Transmit High-priority Buffer Retry
                                                      Counter Register
                0x0058                TXMSGSTS        Transmit Message Status Register
                0x005C                TXHMSGSTS       Transmit High-priority Message Status
                                                      Register
                0x0060                ERRSTS          Error Status Register
                0x0064                ERRCNTR         Error Counter Register
                0x0068~0x00FC         –               Reserved
                0x0100~0x100+(4*N)    AF              Receive Acceptance Filter Register
                0x0140~0x140+(4*N)    AFM             Receive Acceptance Filter Mask Register
                0x0180~0x01FC         –               Reserved
                0x0200                RXB             Receive Buffer/FIFO Window Register
                0x0204                TXB             Transmit Buffer/FIFO Window Register
                0x0208                TXHB            Transmit High-priority Buffer/FIFO
                                                      Window Register
                0x020C                TXBSTS          Transmit Buffer/FIFO Status Register
                0x0210                TXHBSTS         Transmit High-priority Buffer/FIFO Status
                                                      Register
                0x0214                RXBSTS          Receive Buffer/FIFO Status Register


19.2.2 Register Description

                   The following sections describe the CAN register definitions in details.
                   The register type abbreviations are summarized as follows:
                  RO: Read-only
                  R/W: Readable and writable
                  WO: Write-only
               SRST Register（0x0000）
                     The SRST register means software reset register. Writing “1” to bit 0
               initiates a hard reset of the controller, similar to a power-on reset, resetting

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                192(346)
  19 CAN                                                                            19.2 Register Definition



               the entire controller. The definition of SRST register is as shown in Table
               19-2.
               Table 19-2 SRST Register
                Name      Bit         Type    Description                                            Reset
                -         31:1        -       Reserved                                               -
                RstCtrl   0           R/W     Control reset                                          0x0
                                              0: Software reset
                                              1: Hardware reset, reset controller completely



               CMD Register（0x0004）
                    CMD register is used to set operating mode or command mode. The
               definition of CMD register is as shown in Table 19-3.
               Table 19-3 CMD Register
                Name                  Bit        Type         Description                      Reset
                -                     31:1       -            Reserved                         -
                CmdSet                0          R/W          Command                          0x0
                                                              0: Command mode
                                                              1: Working mode



               BRP Register（0x0008）
                   BRP register is used to generate the prescaler value for the TQ clock
               from CANCLK using integer division. The definition of BRP register is as
               shown in Table 19-4.
               Table 19-4 BRP Register
                Name            Bit          Type         Description                          Reset
                -               31:8         -            Reserved                             -
                BRP             7:0          R/W          Baud rate prescalar                  0x0



               BTN Register（0x000C）
                    BTN register defines the nominal or normal bit timing parameters. The
               definition of BTN register is as shown in Table 19-5.
               Table 19-5 BTN Register
                Name              Bit        Type    Description                                     Reset



MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                       193(346)
  19 CAN                                                                            19.2 Register Definition



                -                 31:29       -       Reserved                                      -
                sjw_nom           28:24       R/W     sjw_nom                                       0x0
                -                 23:14       -       Reserved                                      -
                phseg2_nom        13:8        R/W     phseg2_nom                                    0x0
                                                      PHASE_SEG2 window’s width
                phseg1_nom        7:0         R/W     phseg1_nom                                    0x0
                                                      PROP_SEG+PHASE_SEG1 window’s
                                                      width



               BTD Register（0x0010）
                   BTD register defines the bit timing parameters for the allowed optional
               higher data rates in Flexible Data-rate (FDS), and the parameters are
               used during the data phase. The definition of BTD register is as shown in
               Table 19-6.
               Table 19-6 BTD Register
                Name        Bit          Type       Description                                     Reset
                -           31:27        -          Reserved                                        -
                sjw_d       26:24        R/W        sjw_d                                           0x0
                -           23:12        -          Reserved                                        -
                phseg2_d    11:8         R/W        phseg2_d                                        0x0
                                                    PHASE_SEG2 window’s depth
                -           7:4          -          Reserved                                        -
                phseg1_d    3:0          R/W        phseg1_d                                        0x0
                                                    PROP_SEG+PHASE_SEG1 window’s depth



               IS Register（0x0020）
                    IS register indicates the status of various events. The definition of IS
               register is as shown in Table 19-7.
               Table 19-7 IS Register

                BusOffSts    31           RO        Bus off status                                  0x0
                -            30:28        -         Reserved                                        -
                TxMSSts      27           RO        Transmit message successfully status            0x0
                TxMRSts      26           RO        Transmit message retry status                   0x0

                Name         Bit    Type   Description                                       Reset

                -            24     -      Reserved                                          -
                TxHMSSts     23     RO     Transmit high-priority message successfully       0x0
                                           status
                TxHMRSts     22     RO     Transmit high-priority message retry status       0x0
                TxHMFSts     21     RO     Transmit high-priority message failed status      0x0

                -           7:6     -      Reserved                                           -

               RXBCFG Register（0x0040）
                   RXBCFG register defines the size of the receive buffer (in 32-bit
               words). The definition of RXBCFG register is as shown in Table 19-11.
               Table 19-11 RXBCFG Register
                Name         Bit          Type        Description                                 Reset
                RxBEnd       31:16        R/W         Receive buffer ending offset                0x0
                RxBStart     15:0         R/W         Receive buffer start offset                 0x0



               TXBCFG Register（0x0044）
                   TXBCFG register defines the size of the transmit buffer (in 32-bit
               words). The definition of TXBCFG register is as shown in Table 19-12.
               Table 19-12 TXBCFG Register
                Name         Bit          Type       Description                                  Reset
                TxBEnd       31:16        R/W        Transmit buffer ending offset                0x0
                TxBStart     15:0         R/W        Transmit buffer start offset                 0x0



               TXHBCFG Register（0x0048）
                    TXBCFG register defines the size of the transmit high-priority buffer
               (in 32-bit words). The definition of TXHBCFG register is shown in Table
               19-13.
               Table 19-13 TXHBCFG Register
                Name        Bit      Type        Description                                          Reset
                TxHBEnd     31:16    R/W         Transmit high-priority buffer ending offset          0x0



MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                          197(346)
