                TxHBStart   15:0    R/W      Transmit high-priority buffer start offset          0x0



               TXBRETRY Register（0x0050）
                    TXBRETRY register defines the number of TX retries in case of
               transmission errors. The definition of TXBRETRY register is shown in
               Table 19-14.
               Table 19-14 TXBRETRY Register
                Name         Bit      Type       Description                                   Reset
                TxBRetry     31:0     R/W        Transmit buffer retry counter.                0x0
                                                 The retries can be set to
                                                 0: Unlimited times
                                                 1: 1 time
                                                 2: 2 times
                                                 …
                                                 15: 15 times



               TXHBRETRY Register（0x0054）
                   TXBRETRY register defines the number of high-priority TX retries in
               case of transmission errors. The definition of TXHBRETRY register is
               shown in Table 19-15.
               Table 19-15 TXHBRETRY Register

                TxHBRetry   31:0    R/W      Transmit high-priority buffer retry counter.        0x0
                                             The retries can be set to
                                             0: Unlimited times
                                             1: 1 time
                                             2: 2 times
                                             …
                                             15: 15 times



               TXMSGSTS Register（0x0058）
                   TXMSGSTS register records the status of the last transmit message.
               The definition of TXMSGSTS register is shown in Table 19-16.

               ERRSTS Register（0x0060）
                    ERRSTS register records error events defined in the specification,
               and they are cleared until CPU writes "1" to the respective bit. The
               definition of ERRSTS register is as shown in Table 19-18.
               Table 19-18 ERRSTS Register
                Name                 Bit            Type         Description               Reset
                -                    31:5           -            Reserved                  -
                ErrCRC               4              R/W          CRC error                 0x0
                ErrACK               3              R/W          ACK error                 0x0
                ErrFORM              2              R/W          Form error                0x0
                ErrBIT               1              R/W          Bit error                 0x0
                ErrSTUFF             0              R/W          Stuff error               0x0


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                       199(346)
  19 CAN                                                                              19.2 Register Definition




               ERRCNTR Register（0x0064）
                    ERRCNTR register reflects the values of the transmit and receive
               error counters. The transmit and receive error counters increase and
               decrease, respectively, according to the definition in the specification. The
               definition of ERRCNTR register is shown in Table 19-19.
               Table 19-19 ERRCNTR Register
                Name         Bit            Type          Description                            Reset
                -            31:25          -             Reserved                               -
                Tec          24:16          RO            Transmit error coutner                 0x0
                -            15:9           -             Reserved                               -
                Rec          8:0            RO            Receive error counter                  0x0



               AF Register（0x0100~0x100+(4*N)）
                     Each filter has a corresponding bit mask register, which the CPU can
               program to selectively match subsets of the identifier or other fields. Each
               AF is a 32-bit register, and the first filter starts at offset 0x100. Subsequent
               filters are spaced 4 bytes apart. The number of receive filters is N = 1 to
               16. The definition of AF register is as shown in Table 19-20.
               Table 19-20 AF Register
                Name                 Bit           Type       Description                            Reset
                En                   31            R/W        Enable                                 0x0
                                                              0: Invalid
                                                              1: Valid
                IDE                  30            R/W        IDE                                    0x0
                                                              0: Normal frame
                                                              1: Extended frame
                ExtDataLen           29            R/W        Extended data length                   0x0
                                                              0: Match normal frame
                                                              1: Match FD frame
                BasicID              28:18         R/W        Basic ID                               0x0
                IE                   17:0          R/W        ID extension                           0x0



               AFM Register（0x0140~0x140+(4*N)）
                      The mask vector for each receive filter compares/matches only the

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                          200(346)
  19 CAN                                                                          19.2 Register Definition



               bits with a mask of 1 in each filter. Bits with a mask of 0 are treated as
               wildcards and are not compared/matched. Each AFM is a 32-bit register,
               and the first filter starts at offset 0x140. Subsequent filters are spaced 4
               bytes apart. The number of receive filters is N = 1 to 16. The definition of
               AFM register is as shown in Table 19-21.
               Table 19-21 AFM Register
                Name               Bit              Type         Description               Reset
                -                  31:29            -            Reserved                  -
                BasicID            28:18            R/W          Basic ID                  0x0
                IE                 17:0             R/W          ID extension              0x0



               RXB Register（0x0200）
                    RXB register provides indirect access to the RX FIFO buffer. The
               definition of RXB register is as shown in Table 19-22.
               Table 19-22 RXB Register
                Name        Bit          Type      Description                                 Reset
                RxB         31:0         RO        Receive Buffer/FIFO window                  0x0



               TXB Register（0x0204）
                    TXB register provides indirect access to the TX FIFO buffer. The
               definition of TXB register is as shown in Table 19-23.
               Table 19-23 TXB Register
                Name        Bit          Type      Description                                 Reset
                TxB         31:0         WO        Transmit Buffer/FIFO window                 0x0



               TXHB Register（0x0208）
                    TXHB register provides indirect access to the high-priority TX FIFO
               buffer. The definition of TXHB register is as shown in Table 19-24.
               Table 19-24 TXHB Register
                Name      Bit      Type       Description                                        Reset
                TxHB      31:0     WO         Transmit high-priority Buffer/FIFO window          0x0




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                     201(346)
  19 CAN                                                                          19.2 Register Definition



               TXBSTS Register（0x020C）
                   TXBSTS register indicates the current status of the TX FIFO that the
               CPU reads before writing/pushing a newly transmit frame into the FIFO.
               The definition of TXBSTS register is as shown in Table 19-25.
               Table 19-25 TXBSTS Register
                Name        Bit      Type       Description                                         Reset
                TxBWerr     31       RO         txbwerr                                             0x0
                                                If the CPU is written data causing a Tx FIFO
                                                overflow, this bit is set.
                -           30:16    -          Reserved                                            -
                TxBSpace    15:0     RO         txbspace                                            0x0
                                                The CPU will check the space before writing
                                                each newly sent frame.



               TXHBSTS Register（0x0210）
                    TXHBSTS register indicates the current status of high-priority TX
               FIFO that the CPU reads before writing/pushing a newly transmit frame
               into the FIFO. The definition of TXHBSTS register is shown in Table 19-26.
               Table 19-26 TXHBSTS Register
                Name         Bit         Type    Description                                        Reset
                TxHBWerr     31          RO      txhbwerr                                           0x0
                                                 If the CPU is written data causing a TXH
                                                 FIFO overflow, this bit is set.
                -            30:16       -       Reserved                                           -
                TxHBSpace    15:0        RO      txhbspace                                          0x0
                                                 The CPU will check the space before writing
                                                 each newly sent frame.



               RXBSTS Register（0x0214）
                    RXBSTS register indicates the current status of RX FIFO. The
               definition of RXBSTS register is as shown in Table 19-27.
               Table 19-27 RXBSTS Register
                Name               Bit           Type         Description                      Reset
                -                  31:16         -            Reserved                         -
                RxBDepth           15:0          RO           rxbdepth                         0x0

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                       202(346)
  19 CAN                                                                19.3 Driver Function Definition



                                                        Rx FIFO usage level

  20 SD




                                                                   20                 SD

20.1 Introduction
                    Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC includes an Extended APB Slave bus
               interface, which can be interconnected with an SD controller programmed
               with FPGA logic, enabling the implementation of SD peripheral.
                    This SD peripheral operates on the SPI interface protocol, supporting
               basic SPI bus access and SD/MMC memory cards, with the following
               features:
                  Supports memory cards in full SD/MMC formats, including
                   initialization, block read, and block write operations.
                  Supports basic SPI bus access.
                  Built-in 512-byte RX and TX FIFOs.
                  Supports APB bus interface.
                  Independent clocks for the APB interface and the SPI module.
                  Data rate approaching the maximum rate of SD/MMC cards.

20.2 Register Definition

20.2.1 Register Definition
                    The definition of SD register is as shown in Table 20-1. And SD
               register definition is located in extp\sd\sd.h.
               Table 20-1 Register Definition

                0x000              SPI_MASTER_VERSION          SPI Master Version Register
                0x001              SPI_MASTER_CONTROL          SPI Master Control Register

               DIRECT_ACCESS_DATA Register (0x006)
                    The DIRECT_ACCESS_DATA register is used to set TX DATA prior to
               starting a transaction or read RX DATA after completing a transaction. The
               definition of DIRECT_ACCESS_DATA register is shown in Table 20-8.
               Table 20-8 DIRECT_ACCESS_DATA Register
                Name      Bit     Type       Description                                        Reset
                –         31:8    –          Reserved                                           –
                TX_DATA   7:0     WO         Set TX_DATA prior to starting a                    0x0
                                             DIRECT_ACCESS transaction.
                                             Note that the SPI bus has no concept of a read
                                             or write transaction. Thus every
                                             DIRECT_ACCESS transaction transmits data
                                             from the SPI master, and receives data from the
                                             SPI slave.
                RX_DATA   7:0     RO         Read RX_DATA after completing a                    –
                                             DIRECT_ACCESS transaction



               SD_ADDR_7_0 Register (0x007)

MUG1029-1.3E                                                               238(346)
  23 RTOS                                                                     23.1 FreeRTOS




                                                              23              RTOS

                   Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports the following RTOS:
                  FreeRTOS
                  uC/OS-III
                  RT-Thread Nano version
                  RT-Thread standard version
                  Zephyr

23.1 FreeRTOS

23.1.1 Features

                  FreeRTOS is a lite real-time operating system.
                  It offers functions of task management, time management,
                   semaphore, message queue, memory management, recording,
                   software timer, etc. It can basically meet the needs of smaller systems.
                  FreeRTOS is a free operating system. It has features of open source,
                   portability, reducibility, and flexible scheduling policy.
                  FreeRTOS source code is available at FreeRTOS website:
                   http://www.FreeRTOS.org.
                  Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports FreeRTOS.

23.1.2 Version

                   Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports FreeRTOS V10.3.1.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                       239(346)
  23 RTOS                                                                     23.2 uC/OS-III




23.1.3 Configuration

                   Users can modify “FreeRTOSConfig.h” to configure FreeRTOS.

23.2 uC/OS-III

23.2.1 Features

                  The uC/OS-III is an extensible, romable, and preemptive real-time
                   core. There is no limit to the number of managed tasks.
                  uC/OS-III is the third generation core. It provides a real-time core's
                   functions, including resource management, synchronization, and inter-
                   task communication, etc.
                  uC/OS-III also provides features that are not available for the other
                   real-time cores. For example, it can measure operating performance
                   during runtime and send signals or messages to tasks directly. Tasks
                   can also wait for multiple semaphores and message queues
                   simultaneously.
                  uC/OS-III follows the open source Apache license 2.0 and is free for
                   developers to download and use.
                  uC/OS-III source code is available at Micrium website:
                   http://www.micrium.com.
                  Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports uC/OS-III.

23.2.2 Version

                   Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports uC/OS-III V3.03.00.

23.2.3 Configuration

                   Users can modify “uCOS_CONFIG\os_cfg.h” and “oscfg_app.h” to
               configure uC/OS-III.
                   Users can modify “UCOS_BSP\bsp_os.c” and “bsp_os.h” to get
               board-level support.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                       240(346)
  23 RTOS                                                           23.3 RT-Thread Nano Version




23.3 RT-Thread Nano Version

23.3.1 Features

                  RT-Thread Nano is a lite version of hard real-time core.
                  RT-Thread Nano is developed in C language with object-oriented
                   programming ideas, and it is a trimmable, preemptive real-time
                   multitasking RTOS with a good code style.
                  Its memory resource utilization is extremely small and its functions
                   include task processing, software timers, semaphores, mailboxes and
                   real-time scheduling, etc.
                  It follows the open source Apache license 2.0, and RTOS core and all
                   open source components are free to use in commercial products
                   without the need to publish application source code and without
                   potential commercial risk.
                  RT-Thread Nano version source code is available at the RT-Thread
                   website: http://www.micrium.com.
                  Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports RT-Thread Nano version.

23.3.2 Version

                   Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports RT-Thread Nano V3.1.5.

23.3.3 Configuration

                   Users can modify “bsp\config.h” to configure RT-Thead Nano version.
                   Users can modify “bsp\board.c” to get board-level support.

23.4 RT-Thread Standard Version

23.4.1 Features

                  RT-Thread Standard Version is an embedded real-time multi-threaded
                   operating system, with one of its basic features being support for
                   multitasking, implemented through threads.
                  It is primarily written in C language, easy to understand, easy to port.
                  It has a small footprint, low cost, low power consumption, quick
                   startup, and features such as high real-time performance and minimal

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                          241(346)
  23 RTOS                                                       23.4 RT-Thread Standard Version



                   resource usage, making it suitable for various resource-constrained
                   scenarios.
                  It is completely open-source, following the Apache License 2.0 open-
                   source license. It can be used for free in commercial products, and
                   there is no need to disclose private code, eliminating potential
                   business risks.
                  The source code for RT-Thread Standard Version can be downloaded
                   from the RT-Thread website at https://www.rt-thread.org/.
                  Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports RT-Thread Standard Version.

23.4.2 Version

                    Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports RT-Thread standard version：
               3.1.5.

23.4.3 Driver Support

                   For RT-Thread driver support，please contact GOWINSEMI for
               customized Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC.

23.4.4 Development Kit

                    Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC RT-Thread Standard Version software
               development kit: ae350_rtthread_std.zip, located in the RDS software
               installation directory AndeSight_RDS_v511\demo\ae350_rtthread_std.zip.

23.4.5 User Manual

                   For the details of RT-Thread standard version, refer to https://www.rt-
               thread.org/document/site/#/rt-thread-version/rt-thread-standard/README.

23.4.6 Development Tool

               Env Development Tool
                   Env is a development auxiliary tool introduced by RT-Thread,
               providing a compilation and build environment, graphical system
               configuration, and package management functions for projects based on
               the RT-Thread operating system. Its built-in menuconfig offers a simple
               and easy-to-use configuration tool for core, component, and package
               customization, enabling system construction in a modular way.
                   User Manual: https://www.rt-thread.org/document/site/#/development-

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                          242(346)
  23 RTOS                                                         23.4 RT-Thread Standard Version



               tools/env/env.
                   Development Tool: ae350_rtthread_std\tool\env\env-windows-v1.3.5.
               Scons Build Tool
                    Scons is an open-source build system written in the Python language,
               similar to GNU Make. It uses SConstruct and SConscript files instead of
               the Makefile, and these files are also Python scripts that can use standard
               Python syntax for various complex processing. Therefore, SConstruct and
               SConscript files can call standard Python libraries for various complex
               processing, not limited to the rules set by Makefile.
                    User Manual: https://www.rt-thread.org/document/site/#/development-
               tools/build-config-system/SCons.

23.4.7 Build Procedure

               Step 1
                    Unzip Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC RT-Thread Standard Version
               software development kit ae350_rtthread_std.zip.
               Step 2
                   ae350_rtthread_std\tool\env\env-windows-v1.3.5\env.exe or env.bat to
               open the Env development tool.
               Step 3
                    Right-click the title bar of the Env development tool, select "Settings"
               option, as shown in Figure 23-1. Specify the shortcut for the right-click
               menu of the Env console, as shown in Figure 23-2.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                            243(346)
  23 RTOS                                                     23.4 RT-Thread Standard Version



               Figure 23-1 Select Settings




               Figure 23-2 Set Env Console




                    Once the settings are complete, you can launch the Env console from
               the right-click menu in any folder.


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                        244(346)
  23 RTOS                                                      23.4 RT-Thread Standard Version



               Step 4
                    Open the RT-Thread BSP folder, right-click in a blank space, and
               select "ConEmu Here" to open Env. The Env path will automatically switch
               to the current path.
                    After opening the console, you can use the cd command in command
               line mode to navigate to the Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC folder, as shown
               below:
                   > cd gowin-riscv_ae350_soc
               Step 5
                    Assuming the RT-Thread root directory is <RTT_ROOT>, set the
               environment variable to specify the toolchain for building RT-Thread. Use
               the following commands:
                   > set RTT_CC=gcc
                   > set PATH=<RTT_ROOT>\tool\xpack\xpack-riscv-none-embed-gcc-
               10.2.0-1.2\bin;%PATH%
               Step 6
                   Menuconfig is a graphical configuration tool used by RT-Thread to
               configure and tailor the entire system. Use the following command
                   > menuconfig
                   The graphical system configuration for RT-Thread is as shown Figure
               23-3.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                         245(346)
  23 RTOS                                                          23.4 RT-Thread Standard Version



               Figure 23-3 graphical system configuration for RT-Thread




               Step 7
                    Edit the rtconfig.py file, and modify the "EXEC_PATH" to the current
               location of the development kit.
               Step 8
                    Execute the "scons" command to build RT-Thread and generate the
               software programming binary file, as shown in Figure 23-4. Use the
               following command:
                   > scons




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                             246(346)
  23 RTOS                                                                          23.5 Zephyr



               Figure 23-4 Build RT-Thread




               Step 9
                    Execute the "scons -c" command to clean the project. Use the
               following command:
                   > scons -c
                   Or double-click the "cleanup.bat" file to clean up residual files.

23.5 Zephyr

23.5.1 Features

                  Zephyr is an open-source, free RTOS released under the Apache
                   License 2.0.
                  It builts embedded and extensible RTOS optimized for low-power,
                   small-memory microcontroller devices. Zephyr supports various
                   hardware architectures and development boards and can run on
                   system with as little as 8KB of memory.
                  Zephyr follows a comprehensive security development lifecycle,
                   including security verification, fuzz and penetration testing, frequent
                   code reviews, static code analysis, threat modeling and scrutiny, to
                   prevent backdoors in the code.
                  It is highly configurable, allowing applications to include only the
MUG1029-1.3E                                                                            247(346)
  23 RTOS                                                                       23.5 Zephyr



                   required functionality and specify the quantity and size.
                  It allows system resources to be defined at compile time, reducing
                   code size and improving performance.
                  Zephyr source code is available at the Zephyr website
                   https://zephyrproject.org/.
                  Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports Zephyr.

23.5.2 Version

                   Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports Zephyr V 2.4.0.

23.5.3 User Manual

                    For the details of Zephyr, you can see
               https://docs.zephyrproject.org/2.4.0/.

23.5.4 Development Tool

                   Using the terminal tool "Cygwin" of the RDS software, assuming that
               the installation path of the RDS software is <RDS_ROOT>, "Cygwin" is
               located in "<RDS_ROOT>\cygwin\Cygwin.bat".

23.5.5 Build

               Step 1
                    Double click "<RDS_ROOT>\cygwin\Cygwin.bat" to open the terminal
               tool "Cygwin".
               Step 2

                   Assuming that the root directory of Zephyr is <ZEPHYR_ROOT>, in
               the terminal tool "Cygwin", go to <ZEPHYR_ROOT> and set the
               environment variable to specify the software toolchain for building Zephyr.
               The command is as follows:

                   $ cd <ZEPHYR_ROOT>
                   $ source zephyr-env.sh
                   $ export ZEPHYR_TOOLCHAIN_VARIANT='cross-compile'


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                       248(346)
  23 RTOS                                                                                     23.5 Zephyr



                  $ export CROSS_COMPILE=<RDS_ROOT>/toolchains/nds32le-elf-
               mculib-v5/bin/riscv32-elf-
               Note!
               The interface directory between "Cygwin" and the local PC file system is "/cygdrive"; for
               example, if you enter the D drive system in "Cygwin", the command is as follows: $ cd
               /cygdrive/d/
               For example, if <ZEPHYR_ROOT> is E:\ae350_zephyr, it is represented as
               /cygdrive/e/ae350_zephyr, meaning you should run cd /cygdrive/e/ae350_zephyr;
               similarly, if <RDS_ROOT> is D:\Andestech\AndeSight_RDS_v511, it is represented as
               /cygdrive/d/Andestech/AndeSight_RDS_v511, meaning you should set export
               CROSS_COMPILE=/cygdrive/d/Andestech/AndeSight_RDS_v511/toolchains/nds32le-elf-
               mculib-v5/bin/riscv32-elf-.

               Step 3
                  Select a Zephyr application, assuming the directory is
               <ZEPHYR_APP>, e.g. <ZEPHYR_ROOT>\samples\hello_world, and go to
               <ZEPHYR_APP>. The command is as follows:
                    $ cd <ZEPHYR_APP>
               Step 4
                    Create a folder "build" for building the Zephyr application; enter the
               "build" folder and use the following commands:
                    $ mkdir build
                    $ cd build
               Step 5
                   Execute the "cmake" command to specify the AE350 board-level
               device tree to be used to generate the build file as shown below:
                    $ cmake -DBOARD=adp_xc7k_ae350 ../
                  The AE350 board device tree file, which is located at
               <ZEPHYR_ROOT>\boards\riscv\andes_v5\adp_xc7k_ae350.dts, can be
               modified according to the application.
                    For example, the frequency of the used device UART2 is 100MHz and
               the baud rate is 115200, then in the adp_xc7k_ae350.dts file, modify the
               UART2 device tree definition as shown below.

                &uart1 {
                   status = "okay";
                   clock-frequency = <100000000>;                // UCLK frequency is 100MHz


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                     249(346)
  23 RTOS                                                                           23.5 Zephyr



                     current-speed = <115200>;             // Baud rate is 115200
                };



               Step 6
                   The command used to configure the Zephyr core for the selected
               application and specify core features is shown below:
                     $ make menuconfig
                   The graphical interface for Zephyr core configuration is shown in
               Figure 23-5.
               Figure 23-5 Graphical Interface for Zephyr Core Configuration

  24 Protocol Stack Programming                                             24.1 TCP/IP Protocol Stack




                    24              Protocol Stack Programming

                        Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports TCP/IP protocol stack software
                    programming.

24.1 TCP/IP Protocol Stack
                        Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports the open-source TCP/IP protocol
                    stack, LwIP.
                        LwIP protocol stack is a lightweight open-source TCP/IP protocol
                    stack designed for embedded systems and small devices. It aims to
                    provide support for TCP/IP network protocols, enabling embedded
                    systems to communicate with other devices over the network.

24.1.1 Features

                       Lightweight: Occupies minimal memory and processor resources,
                        making it efficient for embedded systems.
                       Trimmable: Allows trimming based on specific requirements, including
                        only necessary protocols and features to reduce storage space and
                        processor overhead.
                       High Performance: Utilizes performance optimization techniques such
                        as zero-copy and event-driven mechanisms to enhance network
                        communication efficiency and throughput.
                       Portability: Demonstrates good portability and can run on various
                        operating systems and hardware platforms, including RTOS like
                        FreeRTOS.
                       Versatility: Supports multiple application protocols. In addition to the
                        TCP/IP protocol stack, it provides implementations of common
                        application layer protocols such as HTTP, SNMP, and MQTT.
                       Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports LwIP protocol stack in modes like
MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                 253(346)
  24 Protocol Stack Programming                                     24.1 TCP/IP Protocol Stack



                        microcontroller, RTOS (FreeRTOS), etc.

24.1.2 Version

                         Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports LwIP protocol stack version
                    2.1.2.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                         254(346)
  25 TinyML Software Programming                                                      25.1 Tflite Micro




        25                  TinyML Software Programming

25.1 Tflite Micro
                       TFLite Micro (TensorFlow Lite Micro) is a lightweight version of
                   TensorFlow Lite, specifically designed for resource-constrained
                   microcontrollers and embedded devices. It is optimized for devices without
                   an operating system or those running a real-time operating system
                   (RTOS).

25.2 Features
                       Can run on microcontrollers with only a few KB of RAM and Flash.
                       The model is highly optimized for memory usage.
                       Supports multiple platforms, including ARM Cortex-M, RISC-V, etc.
                       Compatible with popular embedded development platforms such as
                        Arduino and Zephyr.
                       Supports adding custom operators and kernels.
                       Compatible with models converted to TensorFlow Lite (.tflite) format.
                       Specifically designed for real-time applications, making it ideal for IoT,
                        wearable devices, and other edge AI applications.

25.3 Process
                       Model Training and Conversion
                        -   Trains the model in TensorFlow
                        -   Converts the model to TensorFlow Lite (.tflite) format using
                            TensorFlow Lite Converter
                       Deployment Preparation
                        -   Runs the .tflite model using TFLite Micro's interpreter
                        -   Integrates the model into an embedded application
MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                 255(346)
  25 TinyML Software Programming                                                     25.3 Process



                       Hardware Optimization
                        -   Quantizes the model (e.g., convert FP32 to INT8 or UINT8) to
                            reduce model size and accelerate inference
                        -   Utilizes hardware acceleration when supported

25.3.1 Architecture
                       Interpreter: Executes TensorFlow Lite models
                       Memory Planner: Efficiently allocates and frees memory for
                        intermediate tensors
                       Operator Implementations: Supports fundamental operations such as
                        convolution, pooling, activation functions, etc.

25.3.2 Application Scenarios
                       Wearable devices: Step counting, heart rate monitoring.
                       IoT: Anomaly detection, speech recognition.
                       Robotics: Vision-based navigation, object detection.
                       Consumer electronics: Voice assistants, gesture recognition.




MUG1029-1.3E                                                                            256(346)
  26 Benchmark Programs                                                           26.1 Dhrystone




                                      26           Benchmark Programs

                       Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC supports the following benchmarking
                  programs to measure the hardware's maximum actual runtime
                  performance of the processor, as well as the performance improvement
                  effects of software optimization.
                         Dhrystone
                         CoreMark
                         Whetstone

26.1 Dhrystone

26.1.1 Introduction

                        The main purpose of Dhrystone is to test the processor's performance
                  in integer and logical operations, and the latest version is V2.1, updated in
                  1988.
                       The standard testing method for Dhrystone is how many times the
                  Dhrystone program can run within a unit of time, with performance
                  measured in DMIPS/MHz. MIPS stands for Million Instructions Per
                  Second, representing the number of millions of machine language
                  instructions processed per second, and the D stands for Dhrystone,
                  indicating the MIPS under the Dhrystone standard testing.

26.1.2 Applications

                          RiscV_AE350_SOC provides Dhrystone application programming:
                          …\ref_design\MCU_RefDesign\ae350_dhrystone

26.1.3 Program Running

                          The results of running the Dhrystone application are shown below.

                   Dhrystone Benchmark, Version 2.1 (Language: C)

26.2 CoreMark

26.2.1 Introduction

                      The purpose of CoreMark is to test the performance of a processor.
                  This standard is considered more practically valuable compared to the
                  outdated Dhrystone benchmark.
                       The CoreMark program is written in the C language and includes the
                  following computational rules: listing (searching and sorting), mathematical
                  matrix operations (common matrix calculations), and a state machine
                  (used to determine the presence of valid numbers in input streams), along
                  with CRC. The latest version of the CoreMark program is V1.0.
                      The test method for the CoreMark standard involves running the
                  CoreMark program a certain number of times within a unit of time under
                  specific configuration parameters. Its performance indicator is expressed
                  in CoreMark/MHz, where a higher CoreMark score indicates better
                  performance.

26.2.2 Applications

                          RiscV_AE350_SOC provides CoreMark application programming:
                          …\ref_design\MCU_RefDesign\ae350_coremark

26.2.3 Program Running

                          The results of running the CoreMark application are shown below.

                   The time is from mcycle

26.3 Whetstone

26.3.1 Introduction

                      Whetstone is a comprehensive performance benchmark program
                  primarily used to assess a computer's floating-point arithmetic
                  performance. Its performance is measured in terms of executing a
                  thousand Whetstone instructions per second (KWIPS) or a million
                  Whetstone instructions per second (MWIPS).
                      Whetstone is a synthetic program consisting of multiple code
                  segments, each representing a mix of common operations found in
                  scientific computing applications. These operations include frequently
                  used C functions (such as sin, cos, sqrt, exp, and log), arithmetic
                  operations with both integers and floating-point numbers, array access,
                  conditional branching, and function calls, etc.


MUG1029-1.3E                                                                            260(346)
  26 Benchmark Programs                                                           26.3 Whetstone



                          Currently, Whetstone is primarily used for testing embedded systems.

26.3.2 Applications

                          RiscV_AE350_SOC provides Whetstone application programming:
                          …\ref_design\MCU_RefDesign\ae350_whetstone

                                   27             Application Programs

                        The following sections describe Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC
                    application programs in details and are intended to facilitating users in
                    quickly using Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC software.

27.1 UART

27.1.1 Program Description

                         The UART application program describes how to use the UART
                    interface to implement asynchronous communication, demonstrating that
                    the UART and the PC can send/receive data to/from each other through a
                    terminal emulation program (e.g. HyperTerm, TeraTerm, etc.).
                         After the program starts, UART sends the string "Input ‘G’ to receive a
                    message" to the terminal and waits for the terminal to send data. If the
                    UART receives the character "G" from the terminal, it sends the string
                    "Hello World!", otherwise it continues to wait for the next data transmission
                    from the terminal.

27.1.2 Application Program

                            Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC provides UART application programming:

27.2 GPIO

27.2.1 Program Description

                         The GPIO application program, which describes how to use the GPIO
                    interface, demonstrates GPIO input and output control for a 7-segment
                    display.
                         Upon program initialization, the GPIO ports are configured with input
                    direction for DIP switches and output direction for the 7-segment display.
                    The program also sets up interrupt triggering on the falling edge of the DIP
                    switch input and enables the corresponding interrupts. Once all
                    configurations are complete, the program enters an infinite loop, waiting
                    for user interaction with 1 to 10 DIP switches to trigger interrupts. When an
                    interrupt occurs, two 7-segment displays will display the corresponding
                    digits from 0 to 9.

27.2.2 Application Program

                            Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC provides GPIO application programming:

27.3 I2C

27.3.1 Program Description

                        The I2C application program describes how to use the I2C interface to
                    achieve communication between a master and a slave, demonstrating the
                    I2C master sending 10 bytes of data to the slave and then reading back
                    these 10 bytes of data from the slave to verify the results. This program
                    requires connecting two development boards, one serving as the master
                    and the other as the slave.
                        Initially, it initializes the I2C slave development board and waits for
                    data to be sent by the master. Then, it initializes the I2C master
                    development board, where the master sends 10 bytes of data to the slave.
                    The master subsequently reads back these 10 bytes of data from the
                    slave, checks the results, and if the data matches, the master sends a test
                    passed message via UART.
                        The I2C master and slave development boards must share a common
                    ground.

27.3.2 Application Program

                            Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC provides I2C application programming:
                            …\ref_design\MCU_RefDesign\ae350_demo\src\demo\i2c

                    below.

27.4 SPI

27.4.1 Program Description

                        The SPI application program describes how to achieve
                    communication between a master and a slave using the SPI interface,
                    demonstrating data exchange between the SPI master and slave. This
                    program requires connecting two development boards, one serving as the
                    master and the other as the slave.
                         Upon program initialization, the SPI master configures the interface
                    with an 8-bit data length and a 33MHz clock frequency. Once the
                    configuration is complete, the master sends 8 bytes of data to the slave.
                    Subsequently, the slave reads back these 8 bytes of data and checks
                    whether the results match.

27.4.2 Application Program

27.5 RTC

27.5.1 Program Description

                            The RTC application program describes how to implement timer

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                          267(346)
  27 Application Programs                                                              27.6 PWM



                    interrupts using the RTC interface, demonstrating interrupts of half a
                    second, one second, one minute, one day, and alarm.
                         Upon program initialization, the "rtc_time" is set to 0, and the
                    "alarm_time" is set to 1 minute. When "rtc_time" counts for 1 second, it
                    triggers the one-second interrupt, and UART sends the message "Second
                    interrupt done". Similarly, when "rtc_timer" reaches the value of
                    "alarm_time", it triggers the alarm interrupt, and UART sends the message
                    "Alarm interrupt done".

27.5.2 Application Program

                            RiscV_AE350_SOC provides RTC application programming:
                            …\ref_design\MCU_RefDesign\ae350_demo\src\demo\rtc

                    notes.
                        After program initialization, UART sends instructions to the terminal
                    and waits for data input. If the terminal sends numbers 1 to 7, PWM
                    outputs corresponding frequencies ("Do" to "Si") to the speaker to play

27.7 WDT and PIT Timer

27.7.1 Program Description

                        The WDT and PIT Timer application program describes how to use
                    the WDT and PIT Timer interfaces to implement system reset and locking,

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                269(346)
  27 Application Programs                                                     27.7 WDT and PIT Timer



                    demonstrating the system reset when the WDT is paused.
                         After WDT initialization, the PIT Timer periodically restarts the WDT.
                    After the PIT Timer restarts the WDT 10 times, PIT Timer stops. Then,
                    when the WDT times out, the system restarts. If the WDT has an interrupt
                    stage, the program receives an NMI interrupt after the timeout, sends an
                    error message, and waits for the system reset.

27.7.2 Application Program

                        RiscV_AE350_SOC provides WDT and PIT Timer application
                    programming:
                            …\ref_design\MCU_RefDesign\ae350_demo\src\demo\wdt_pit

27.8 PLMT

27.8.1 Program Description

                         The PLMT application program describes how to activate interrupts
                    related to machine mode, enabling machine mode timers and machine
                    mode software interrupts.
                         For the machine mode timer interrupt, the period is set to 2 seconds.
                    If an interrupt occurs, it sets the trigger flag for the machine mode software
                    interrupt.
                         For the machine mode software interrupt, when the trigger flag for the
                    machine mode software interrupt is set by the machine mode timer
                    interrupt, the machine mode software interrupt is activated.
                         Once the machine mode software interrupt is activated, it outputs
                    information about the active status of the machine mode timer every 2
                    seconds. Additionally, buttons 1, 2, and 3 control LEDs 1, 2, and 3,
                    respectively.

27.8.2 Application Program

                            Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC provides PLMT application programming:
                            …\ref_design\MCU_RefDesign\ae350_demo\src\demo\plmt

27.11 HSP

27.11.1 Program Description

                        The HSP application program describes hardware stack protection
                    and logging mechanisms.

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                              273(346)
  27 Application Programs                                                                27.11 HSP



                         It uses the Tower of Hanoi game to illustrate the hardware stack
                    protection features. Initially, it runs a scenario with hardware stack logging
                    enabled, obtaining the stack pointer for recursive Tower of Hanoi moves.
                    Then, it runs a scenario with hardware stack protection enabled, lowering
                    the stack boundary value to induce a stack overflow exception while
                    continuing to move the Tower of Hanoi.

27.12 PFM

27.12.1 Program Description

                        The PFM application program describes how to use basic RISC-V
                    performance counters such as "mcycle" and "minstret" for analysis.
                         Using the "mcycle" and "minstret" hardware performance monitors, it
                    measures the performance of an MCU using a Fibonacci program. It
                    demonstrates two measurement methods: one that uses counter
                    differences and another that clears the counters and directly uses their
                    values.

27.13 PLIC

27.13.1 Program Description

                         The PLIC application program, which involves non-vectored interrupt
                    handling, describes how to activate the Machine Mode and PLIC-related
                    interrupts. It demonstrates the activation of Machine Mode Timer, Machine
                    Mode Software, and PLIC PIT Timer interrupts.
                         For the machine mode timer interrupt, the period is set to 2 seconds.
                    If an interrupt occurs, it sets the trigger flag for the machine mode software
                    interrupt.
                         The PLIC PIT Timer interrupt has a period of 200 milliseconds. If this
                    interrupt occurs, it also sets the trigger flag for the machine mode software
                    interrupt.
                         When both the machine mode timer interrupt and the PLIC PIT Timer
                    interrupt have set the trigger flag for the machine mode software interrupt,
                    the machine mode software interrupt is activated.
                        The machine mode software interrupt sends messages every 2
                    seconds indicating the active status of the machine mode timer and every
                    200 milliseconds indicating the active status of the PLIC PIT Timer.

27.13.2 Application Program

                            Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC provides PLIC application programming:
                            …\ref_design\MCU_RefDesign\ae350_demo\src\demo\plic

27.14 Powerbrake

27.14.1 Program Description

                        The Powerbrake application program describes a hardware
                    performance tuning mechanism.
                        The MCU is configured to run a digital game (0 => 9) under two
                    conditions: the lowest performance and the highest performance. When
                    the MCU is configured for the lowest performance, the loop count is
                    greater than the loop count when the MCU is configured for the highest
                    performance.

27.16 Cache

27.16.1 Program Description

                       The Cache application program describes the hardware cache
                    mechanism.
                         It runs code modifications in memory that can be cached to achieve
                    data consistency. First, it checks cache features, and if the features exist,
                    it enables instruction and data caching. Then, it modifies a global variable
                    named "g_selfmodify" in the data cache and uses the "fence.i" instruction
                    for memory consistency. Finally, it checks the correctness of
                    "g_selfmodify."

27.16.2 Application Program

                            Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC provides Cache application programming:
                            …\ref_design\MCU_RefDesign\ae350_demo\src\demo\cache

27.16.3 Program Running

                        The results of running the Cache application program are shown
                    below.

                      It's a L1 Cache demo.
                      MCU supports CCTL operation
                      MCU supports CCTL auto-increment
                      The L1C ICache sets = 256
                      The L1C ICache ways = 4
                      The L1C ICache line size = 32
                      The L1C DCache sets = 256
                      The L1C DCache ways = 4
                      The L1C DCache line size = 32
                      Enable L1C I cache
                      Enable L1C D cache
                      Execute original self modify code.
                      I/D cache flush

27.17 Cache Lock

27.17.1 Program Description

                         The Cache Lock application program describes the hardware cache
                    locking mechanism.
                         It runs code modifications in memory that can be cached to achieve
                    data consistency and explains how to lock the modified code into the
                    instruction cache. First, it checks cache features, and if the features exist,
                    it enables instruction and data caching. Then, it runs the "A" patch code,
                    modifying a global variable named "g_selfmodify" and uses CCTL
                    instructions for memory consistency, both with and without locking the
                    patch code. Next, it runs a 32KB nop instruction in the instruction cache to
                    flush the "A" patch code. Finally, it runs another "B" patch code, modifying
                    "g_selfmodify," and checks the correctness of "g_selfmodify" to complete
                    cache locking.

27.17.2 Application Program

                        Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC provides Cache Lock application
                    programming:
                            …\ref_design\MCU_RefDesign\ae350_demo\src\demo\cache_lock

27.17.3 Program Running

                        The results of running the Cache Lock application program are shown
                    below.

                      It's a L1 Cache Lock Mechanism demo.
                      MCU supports cache lock feature.
                      ==============demo cache unlock===============
                      Run unlock self modify code, expect g_selfmodify=8888888.....
                      Run self modify code to set g_selfmodify = 7777777.....
                      Run 32k NOP buf to full-fill Icache to flush the unlocked Icache line of
                      self modify code.....

27.20 Interrupt Priority

27.20.1 Program Description

                        The Interrupt Priority application program describes how to use
                    multiple peripheral interrupts.
                         It enables both GPIO buttons and PIT Timer, with GPIO interrupt
                    priority set higher than PIT Timer interrupt priority. The PIT Timer
                    generates an interrupt every 4 seconds. When a GPIO button interrupt
                    occurs, it interrupts the ongoing PIT Timer interrupt, and the GPIO
                    interrupt handler is executed.

27.24 PMP and Privilege

27.24.1 Program Description

                         The PMP and Privilege application program describes how to use the
                    correct privilege modes to protect physical memory.
                        First, in machine mode, it initializes a memory region "PA" using PMP
                    configuration. Then it switches from Machine mode to Supervisor mode

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                             288(346)
  27 Application Programs                                                   27.24 PMP and Privilege



                    and further switches to User mode. When the program is running in User
                    mode and attempts to Store/Fetch data from the "PA" memory region
                    without Write/Execute privileges, it will generate an instruction access
                    exception.

27.24.2 Application Program

                        Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC provides PMP and Privilege and
                    application programming:
                            …\ref_design\MCU_RefDesign\ae350_privilege

27.24.3 Program Running

                        The results of running PMP and Priviledge application program are
                    shown below.

                      MCU supports N-Extension feature.
                      PMP uses NAPOT scheme!.
                      Machine mode switches to supervisor mode.
                      =======================================================
                      ========
                      It's running in supervisor mode.
                      Hello World [SV Mode]!
                      Supervisor mode PASS.
                      =======================================================
                      ========
                      Supervisor mode switches to user mode.
                      =======================================================
                      ========
                      It's running in user mode.
                      It's a Physical Memory Protection demo.
                      Try to store a word to a region allowing in user or machine mode.
                      You should see the [Store Pass] message ...
                      [Store Pass]
                      Next, try to store a word to a region requiring in machine mode.
                      You should see the [Data Store Access Fault] message ...

27.31 Flash Memory R/W

27.31.1 Program Description

                        The Flash Memory R/W application program describes how to use
                    Flash as a memory peripheral for read, write and erase operations.
                         After Flash is initialized, it switches to the memory access mode of
                    read, write, and erase. Flash erases a segment of address space with the
                    starting address and size specified, and then writes data at the specified
                    address. Finally, the data at the specified address is read and compared
                    with the written data to verify the read and write.

27.31.2 Application Program

                        Gowin RiscV_AE350_SOC provides Flash Memory R/W application
                    programming:

27.34 LwIP

27.34.1 Program Description

                        The LwIP application demonstrates how to use the LwIP protocol
                    stack for communication, including the following scenarios:
                           Standalone mode: Using the LwIP protocol stack to execute the ping
                            command for network communication testing.
                           TCP communication: The host (PC) acts as the server, and the
                            embedded device acts as the client.
                           TCP communication: The host (PC) acts as the client, and the
                            embedded device acts as the server.
                           UDP communication.
                           FreeRTOS mode: Using the LwIP protocol stack to execute the ping
                            command for network communication testing
                         The server and the client are connected via network cable. The server
                    software (ATKKPING) is configured with the target host address:
                    192.168.31.88 to ping the client to check network connectivity.

  28 Compiler Built-in Functions                                             28.1 Built-in Function Definition




                      28               Compiler Built-in Functions

                           In a compiler, built-in functions are the functions available in a given
                     programming language, and their implementation is handled by the
                     compiler itself. If a function is a built-in function, its code is typically
                     inserted inline, avoiding the overhead of function calls and allowing
                     efficient machine instructions to be generated for this function.
                         The compiler built-in functions in the RDS software are provided for
                     users who do not wish to engage in assembly programming, including
                     operating system engineers, and they encompass all operations that the
                     compiler cannot directly produce.

28.1 Built-in Function Definition

28.1.1 Built-in Function RV32 "I"

                         For the built-in function of RV32 "I" basic integer instructions, their
                     prototypes, aliases, mapped instructions, and schedulability (whether the
                     compiler can schedule the function) as shown in Table 28-1.
                     Table 28-1 Built-in Function of RV32 "I" Instructions

                                         SETMASK, const unsigned int CSRN)

                       Alias                 void __nds__set_csr_bits(unsigned long SETMASK, const
                                             unsigned int CSRN)
                       Description           This intrinsic inserts a csrs or csrsi pseudo-instruction into the
                                             instruction stream. The content of the source operand SRC will
                                             be used to set bits in the CSR csrn.

                       Return Value          None
                       Privilege Level       ALL
                       Example               #include <nds_intrinsic.h>

                       Description       This intrinsic inserts an AMOSWAP.W instruction into the

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                      339(346)
  29 PLIC/PLIC_SW Built-in Function                                     29.1 Built-in Function Definition




        29                  PLIC/PLIC_SW Built-in Function

                         The RDS software compiler provides access to the PLIC/PLIC_SW
                    built-in functions. The macro definition symbols "NDS_PLIC_BASE" and
                    "NDS_PLIC_SW_BASE" are used to enable the PLIC/PLIC_SW built-in
                    functions. In order to activate the PLIC/PLIC_SW built-in functions, the
                    PLIC/PLIC_SW base address must be defined and allocated before the
                    platform header file is included.
                          For example:
                          #define NDS_PLIC_BASE 0xE4000000
                          #define NDS_PLIC_SW_BASE 0xE6400000
                          #include <nds_v5_platform.h>

29.1 Built-in Function Definition
                          The definition of PLIC/PLIC_SW built-in function is as follows.

  Prototype
  void __nds__plic_set_feature (unsigned int FEATURE)

  void __nds__plic_set_threshold (unsigned int THRESHOLD)

  void __nds__plic_set_priority (unsigned int SOURCE, unsigned int PRIORITY)

  void __nds__plic_set_pending (unsigned int SOURCE)

  void __nds__plic_enable_interrupt (unsigned int SOURCE)

  void __nds__plic_disable_interrupt (unsigned int SOURCE)

  unsigned int __nds__plic_claim_interrupt(void)



MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                   340(346)
  29 PLIC/PLIC_SW Built-in Function                                         29.2 Built-in Function Description




  void __nds__plic_complete_interrupt(unsigned int SOURCE)

  void __nds__plic_sw_set_threshold (unsigned int THRESHOLD)

  void __nds__plic_sw_set_priority (unsigned int SOURCE, unsigned int PRIORITY)

  void __nds__plic_sw_set_pending (unsigned int SOURCE)

  void __nds__plic_sw_enable_interrupt (unsigned int SOURCE)

  void __nds__plic_sw_disable_interrupt (unsigned int SOURCE)

  unsigned int __nds__plic_sw_claim_interrupt(void)

  void __nds__plic_sw_complete_interrupt(unsigned int SOURCE)


29.2 Built-in Function Description

29.2.1 PLIC Built-in Function

                        The following sections describe PLIC's built-in function definitions in
                    details.
                    __nds__plic_set_feature
                        The detailed description of the __nds__plic_set_feature function is as
                    shown in Table 29-1.
                    Table 29-1 __nds_plic_set_feature
                     Prototype         void __nds__plic_set_feature (unsigned int FEATURE)
                     Description       This function sets the feature for PLIC.
                     Parameter         FEATURE is the feature that will be enabled for PLIC.
                                       It includes the following options:
                                       NDS_PLIC_FEATURE_PREEMPT to enable priority-based
                                       preemption
                                       NDS_PLIC_FEATURE_VECTORED to enable vectored mode
                                       for PLIC
                     Return Value      None



                    __nds__plic_set_threshold
                        The detailed description of the __nds__plic_set_threshold function is
                    as shown in Table 29-2.

MUG1029-1.3E                                                                                        341(346)
  29 PLIC/PLIC_SW Built-in Function                                           29.2 Built-in Function Description



                    Table 29-2 __nds_plic_set_threshold
                     Prototype         void __nds__plic_set_threshold (unsigned int THRESHOLD)
                     Description       This function sets the interrupt priority threshold for PLIC.
                     Parameter         THRESHOLD is the interrupt priority threshold for PLIC. The
                                       greater the value is, the higher the threshold is.
                     Return Value      None



                    __nds__plic_set_priority
                        The detailed description of the __nds__plic_set_priority function is as
                    shown in Table 29-3.
                    Table 29-3 __nds_plic_set_priority
                     Prototype        void __nds__plic_set_priority (unsigned int SOURCE, unsigned
                                      int PRIORITY)
                     Description      This function sets the priority level for the specified PLIC interrupt.
                     Parameter        SOURCE is the ID number of the specified interrupt.
                                      PRIORITY is the priority level.
                     Return Value     None



                    __nds__plic_set_pending
                        The detailed description of the __nds__plic_set_pending function is
                    as shown in Table 29-4.
                    Table 29-4 __nds_plic_set_pending
                     Prototype           void __nds__plic_set_pending (unsigned int SOURCE)
                     Description         This function sets the pending status for the specified PLIC
                                         interrupt.
                     Parameter           SOURCE is the IDnumber of the specified interrupt.
                     Return Value        None



                    __nds__plic_enable_interrupt
                         The detailed description of the __nds__plic_enable_interrupt function
                    is as shown in Table 29-5.

                        The detailed description of the __nds__plic_complete_interrupt
                    function is as shown in Table 29-7.
                    Table 29-7 __nds_plic_complete_interrupt
                     Prototype        void __nds__plic_complete_interrupt(unsigned int SOURCE)
                     Description      SOURCE is the ID number of the specified interrupt.
                     Parameter        This function sets the specified interrupt as completed so that the
                                      next such interrupt can be forwarded to the PLIC and processed.
                     Return Value     None

                    in details.
                    __nds__plic_sw_set_threshold
                        The detailed description of the built-in __nds__plic_sw_set_threshold
                    function is as shown in Table 29-8.
                    Table 29-8 __nds_plic_sw_set_threshold
                     Prototype        void __nds__plic_sw_set_threshold (unsigned int THRESHOLD)
                     Description      This function set the interrupt priority threshold for PLIC_SW.
                     Parameter        THRESHOLD is the interrupt priority threshold for PLIC_SW. The
                                      larger the value is, the higher the threshold is.
                     Return Value     None
